                              * A 312- creation of New All india Service common to centre & states
                              * Allows Parliament to create new All India Services (AIS)... if Rajya Sabha approves by a 2/3rd majority…
                              * A. 352, 356, 360- if LS is dissolved, Proclamation remains effective even if RS has passed it. 
                              * Utility of RS- It is second house not secondary house- Sardar Patel
                              * Not a Weak House unlike Britain – RS debated and delayed the Farm Laws Repeal Bill (2021) instead of passing it hastily.
                              * Legislative Scrutiny – Citizenship Amendment Bill (2016) – Stalled in RS, forcing govt to reintroduce and pass it in 2019 after securing a stronger LS majority.
                              * Eminent Persons' Role – P.T. Usha (2022) and Ilaiyaraaja (2022) brought sports and arts expertise to Parliament; Sudha Murty
                              * Federal Equilibrium – RS amplified state concerns on GST revenue (2020) against central decisions. 
                              * Continuity in Governance – Ensured governance stability even during LS transitions, e.g., Emergency (1975-77).
                              * Concerns About Rajya Sabha
                              * Backdoor Entry – Arun Jaitley (2014) and Manmohan Singh (1991-2014) held key posts despite LS losses
                              * Overriding powers of LS rendering RS a stepney tyre
                              * Joint Sitting (POTA, 2002) – Used to push a controversial law despite RS objections.
                              * Money Bill Misuse– Aadhar bill controversy in 2016; Finance Act, 2017 Introduced sweeping changes (like tribunal restructuring) as a Money Bill to avoid RS debate.
                              * PM Without LS Mandate– (Indira Gandhi, 1966) Became PM despite not being an LS MP initially.
                              * No-Confidence Motion Avoidance (2023) – Government with LS majority ignored demands for discussion on key issues, blocking opposition motions
                              * Membership of parliament 
                              * Qualifications as per constitution
                              * Citizen
                              * Age- 25 for LS, 30 for RS
                              * Make & subscribe oath or affirmations before authorization by EC 
                              * Bear true faith & allegiance to Consti. 
                              * Faithfully discharge duty 
                              * Uphold Sovereignty & integrity 
                              * Penalty of ₹500/day for sitting/voting if:
                              * Oath not taken after election in front of president
                              * Membership disqualified
                              * Prohibited by parliamentary law
                              * No privileges, voting, or participation without oath.
                              * He must possess other qualifications as per parlia law.
                              * Additional in RPA 1951
                              * A person must be registered as an elector in any parlia constituency in India to contest elections for both the LS and the RS (2003 amendment)."
                              * Reserved community member for candidature in reserve constituency.
                              * Disqualifications 
                              * Constitutional provision ( Article 102) 
                              * Not indian citizen/ Citizenship acquired of foreign country
                              * Office of Profit ( not defined by Constitution or RPA), except minister ( A. 102 ) , MLA, MLC (A.191 )
                              * Article 103 - President  is the final authority to decide whether an MP holds OoP. acts on binding Advice of ECI. 
                              * Parlia (Prevention of Disqualification) Act, 1959 exempts several posts from disqualification on the grounds of ‘Office of Profit’
                              * Pradyut Bordoloi vs Swapan Roy (2001) = 4 principles for office of profit definition
                              * Govt appoint, remove
                              * Remuneration is provided by govt
                              * Has govt powers ?
                              * EN to influence by patronage ? 
                              * Unsound mind (declared by Court)
                              * Undischarged insolvent
                              * RPA act 1951
                              * Guilty of election offences and corrupt practices during elections
                              * Failure to lodge election expenditure in time
                              * Conviction for promoting enmity, bribery 
                              * Must not be Punished - preaching social crimes, bribery, dowry
                              * Convicted and sentenced to imprisonment of 2 years or more is disqualified from the date of conviction and remains disqualified for the duration of the sentence, plus an additional 6 years after release.”
                              * But preventive detention is not disqualification 
                              * No interest in govt service, works, contracts
                              * Office of Profit in a corporation where the govt has 25% share or Must not be director, managing agent.
                              * On questions of disqualifying MPs, the President's decision is final but must be based on the binding opinion of the ECI. This decision is subject to judicial review.
                              * 10th schedule- Disqualification on Defection ground
                              * Voting contrary party direction
                              * Independent elected members joined any party.
                              * Nominated member join party after 6 months expiry
                              * membership of party on whose ticket won, given up voluntarily
                              * On disqual based on defection (52nd CAA, 10th sch), Speaker in LS and Chairman in RS decision is final and binding. But it is subject to JR. 
                              * Rules of disqualification by Speaker
                              * Rule 373: Speaker can order a member to withdraw for the day's session for disorderly conduct.
                              * Rule 374: Speaker can name a member, and the House may suspend them for the session.
                              * Rule 374A: Automatic suspension for five sittings or the session if a member disrupts proceedings after being named.
                              * Vacating seats ( A.101 - dual membership, resignation, absent, disqual)
                              * Double Membership (as per RPA 1951)
                              * If elected to both Houses of parliament; must choose within 10 days, else RS seat vacated.
                              * If a sitting member is elected to another House, the first seat is vacated.
                              * Holding 2 seats in 1 house? Must choose one, else both are vacated.
                              * MP + MLA? Must resign from state legislature within 14 days, else the MP seat vacated. 
                              * Disqualification: Under Consti (Article 102), RPA & 10th Schedule → Seat vacated
                              * Resignation: Speaker (LS) /Chairman (RS) decides acceptance/ rejection
                              * Absence: 60 days without permission → Seat vacated (in 60 days duration, prorogue/adjournment period is excluded).
                              * Other cases
                              * Election void ( as per HC )- if a disqualified person is elected to parliament, the RPA act provides for the High court to declare election void (not consti)- Appeal in SC. 
                              * Expelled from parliament 
                              * Elected as Prez / VP / governor
                              * Legal protection against disqualification
                              * RPA 1951
                              * Section 8- avoid immediate disqualification to discharge pending duty. 
                              * Section 11- EC may record reason, either allow, reduce sentence. Ex - Sikkim CM S. tamang case 
                              * In Lily Thomas vs Union of India (2013), the SC ruled that MPs/MLAs convicted with a sentence of ≥2 years are immediately disqualified, and a stay on conviction (not just the sentence) is required to prevent disqualification.
 
Presiding officers
                              1. Speaker (A. 93) 
                              1. Origin = Montague Chelmsford reforms
                              * Bfr nomenclature = Prez, depy prez
                              * GoI act 1935 changed name
                              * Notable figures:
                              * Frederik Whyte & Sachidanand Sinha – First appointed Speaker & Deputy Speaker.
                              * Vithalbhai Patel (1925) – First elected Speaker.
                              * Sachidananda sinha- Constituent assembly
                              * GV Mavalankar & Ananthasayanam Ayyangar – First Speaker & Deputy Speaker of LS after independence
                              2. Election & Tenure- Article 93: Speaker elected by Lok Sabha members; President fixes election date, election through a simple majority vote. 
                              3. Sources of Power: Constitution, Parliamentary convention, LS conduct rules.
                              4. Deputy Speaker: Not mandatory to elect simultaneously.
                              5. No separate oath for Speaker, unlike PM/Ministers.
                              6. Vacancy:
                              * Ceases to be a LS member.
                              * Resigns (writes to Deputy Speaker).
                              * Removal Process- Effective majority (Article 94).
                              * Initiation of Resolution: Only in Lok Sabha.
                              * 14 days’ notice required; must be passed by effective majority.
                              * Rajya Sabha has no role
                              * No specific grounds mentioned in the Constitution
                              * Cant preside when removal motion is underway.
                              7. Dissolution of LS? The Speaker continues until a new LS is elected.
                              8. No time limit/process specified for Speaker election—left to legislatures.
                              9. Role, Powers & Functions
                              * Head & representative of Lok Sabha.
                              * Guardian of members’ privileges; final authority on decisions.
                              * Maintains order & decorum.
                              * Final interpreter of Constitution & LS rules.
                              * Floor test - While House in session, the speaker can call for floor test. But when not in session, ONLY PREZ can call a special session for floor test. Floor tests are conducted in Lok Sabha and State Assemblies. Rajya Sabha does not conduct floor tests since the Union government’s majority is determined in Lok Sabha.
                              * Adjourns/suspends House due to lack of quorum.
                              * Casting vote in case of a tie. No vote in first instance
                              * Presides over joint sittings (for bill deadlocks).
                              * Allows secret sittings (excludes outsiders).
                              * Decides Money Bill status.
                              * Rules on defection cases under 10th Schedule (subject to judicial review).
                              * Ex-officio chairman of the parliamentary group & presiding officers' conference.
                              * Supervises and appoints chairman of all parliamentary committees of LS, chairs Business Advisory, Rules & General Purpose Committees.
                              10. Provisions ensuring Independence & Impartiality
                              * Security of tenure: 14 days notice -> 50 members support -> Removed by effective majority. 
                              * Salary from Consolidated Fund, not subject to annual vote.
                              * Work/conduct cannot be criticized, except via substantive motion.
                              * Controls LS proceedings- not under judicial review.
                              * Casting a vote ensures neutrality (cannot vote otherwise).
                              * High precedence- 6th: Equal to CJI, above ministers (except PM/Deputy PM).
                              * Resignation: Speaker → Deputy Speaker; Deputy Speaker → Speaker.
                              * British Practice: Speaker resigns from party; remains neutral for life. "once a speaker, always a speaker"
 Sanjeeva Reddy: Only unopposed post-independence President.
                                 11. Kihoto Hollohan case 1992 - anti defection law validity upheld: But Speaker order is subjected to judicial review 
                                 12. Keisham Meghachandra Singh vs speaker 2020 - disqualification under defection ( 10th schedule) plea must be cleared by Speaker within 3 months
                                 2. Deputy speaker (article 93)
                                 * Election & Removal: Elected by LS; Speaker sets the election date. 
                                 * Vacates if: Ceases to be LS member, resigns, or removed by effective majority after 14-day notice.
                                 * Authority & Role:
                                 * Not subordinate to the Speaker, directly responsible to LS.
                                 * Joint Sitting- Presides in Speaker’s absence; if unavailable, RS Deputy Chairman takes over.
                                 * Cannot preside when facing removal.
                                 * Special privilege: Becomes committee chairman if appointed.
                                 * Political Convention (Since 11th LS)
                                 * Speaker → Ruling party, Deputy Speaker → Main opposition (under parliamentary convention) 
                                 3. Speaker pro tem (P = prez appoint)
                                 1. Selected by the Parliamentary Affairs Minister from a senior LS member list prepared by the Legislative Section.
                                 2. Requires President’s approval.
                                 3. Powers: Holds full powers of  Speaker , presides first Lok Sabha sitting (temporary post) 
                                 4. Duties: Administers oath, enables Speaker election (not the President).
                                 * In India, the oath or affirmation to newly elected Members of Parliament (MPs) is administered by the President of India or a person appointed by the President, typically the Pro-tem Speaker for the Lok Sabha and the Chairman (Vice-President) for the Rajya Sabha, as per Article 99 of the Constitution.
                                 4. Secretary general
                                 1. Admin head ( parliament secretariat )
                                 2. S= speaker appoints consulting with PM, opposition.
                                 3. Answerable -only speaker
                                 4. Authenticate bill in absence of speaker 
                                 5. Remain till 60 years of age 
                                 5. Panel of chairpersons- max 10 members appointed by Speaker. Any can preside in Speaker/Deputy Speaker’s absence (not vacancy) 
                                 1. Vacancy: If both offices are vacant, Speaker’s duties are performed by President-appointed members.
                                 6. Chairman of RS (article 64) 
                                 1. Presiding officer, but not a member of the House so No voting rights.
                                 2. Vice President = Ex Officio Chairman (article 64)
                                 3. Lacks two powers of Speaker:
                                 * Cannot decide if a bill is a money bill.
                                 * Cannot preside over joint sittings.
                                 4. Rulings are binding, and cannot be questioned/criticized.
                                 5. Removal: Effective majority (Article 67).
                                 * Initiation of Resolution only in Rajya sabha
                                 * 14 days notice, must be passed by effective majority 
                                 * LS must approve by simple majority. 
                                 * No particular grounds of removal in constitution unlike Prez (violation of constitution) 
                                 6. Salary: Fixed by Parliament, charged on Consolidated Fund of India. 
                                 7. Deputy chairman RS (Art 89)
                                 1. Only constitutional posts whose elections & removal take place exclusively in RS.
                                 2. Elected by RS members.
                                 3. Removal: 
                                 * Initiation of Resolution → Only in Rajya Sabha.
                                 * 14-day notice required before introducing the resolution.
                                 * Effective Majority → Must be passed by a majority of the total strength of Rajya Sabha.
                                 * No role of LS. 
                                 4. Resigns by writing to the Chairman.
                                 5. Vacates office on ceasing to be a member.
                                 8. Panel of Vice Chairperson of RS- same as LS
                                 9. Joint session @ midnight
                                 1. Celebratory nature, only special occasions. 
                                 2. Only 3 since independence
                                 * 1992- 50th anniversary (quit ind)
                                 * 1997 - 50th anniversary (independence )
                                 * GST rollout 2017
                                 10. Leaders in parliament
                                 1. Leader of House (Prescribed by Rules of Procedure of resp houses)
                                 * Lok Sabha: The PM or a senior minister appointed by the ruling party.
                                 * Leader of the House in RS: Typically the Union Minister of Parliamentary Affairs or another senior minister nominated by govt.
                                 * US = majority leader
                                 * Head of house = speaker 
                                 2. Leader of Opposition (Parliamentary statute)
                                 *  Recognised under Salary & allowance of leaders of oppsn act 1977
                                 * At Least 10% seats
                                 * Functions
                                 * Constructive criticism- As LoP, Atal Bihari Vajpayee (1977-79) – Criticized Emergency excesses while supporting democratic restoration.
                                 * Alternate govt-As a LoP- L.K. Advani (1991-93) – Strengthened BJP’s opposition role, which later led to the formation of the BJP-led NDA government in 1998..
                                 * Checking Govt actions- Mallikarjun Kharge (2014-19) – Held the Modi government accountable on issues like demonetization and GST.
                                 * Salary, allowance, facilities + Order of Precedence, the Leader of the Opposition in both LS and RS holds the 7th rank, equivalent to a Cabinet Minister
                                 11. Whip (Not mentioned anywhere- based on convention)
                                 1. Used in Parliamentary parlance for floor management 
                                 2. Neither in rules nor in parliamentary statue, 
                                 3. Its written order (not oral) 
                                 4. Inherit = colonial rule
                                 5. Political party appointed to serve as assistant floor leader. 
                                 6. No whip - prez election
                                 7. Can remove - party, not house
                                 8. Ensures member attendance, secures support, regulates behavior; non-compliance leads to disciplinary action.  
                                 9. Types - 
                                 * One-Line Whip: Informs members of a vote (abstention allowed) 
                                 * Two-Line Whip: Requires MPs to be present, but does not mandate to vote.  
                                 * Three-Line Whip: The strictest, instructing members to vote in alignment with the party. 
                                 10. Govt’s chief whip - 
                                 *  In LS: The Minister of Parliamentary Affairs 
                                 *  In Rajya Sabha: Minister of State for Parliamentary Affairs
 
GoI (Transaction of Business) Rules, 1961 and GoI (Allocation of Business) Rules 1961,
                                 * By Prez, Art 77
                                 * All executive action of GoI shall be expressed to be taken in the name of the President.
                                 * The President shall make rules for the more convenient transaction of the business of the GoI, and for the allocation among Ministers of the said business.
                                 * Ministries/Departments of Govt = created by the President on advice -PM under these Rules.
                                 * Each department is generally headed by a Secretary who assists the Minister in policy matters and general administration.
                                 * Cabinet secretariat = facilitate smooth transaction in min/ dept
                                 * Headed by Cabinet Secretary
                                 * Administers GoI (Transaction & Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961.
                                 * Not a ministry- under the direct authority PMO.
                                 * Cabinet Secretary chairs the Civil Services Board (CSB), which ensures a minimum tenure of 2 years for officers (recommended by the 2nd ARC and Hota Committee). (Otoh, chief secretary does not have fixed tenure)
                                 * National authority of chemical weapons formed by a resolution and headed under cabinet secretariat. Then became statutory.
 
Sessions of parliament
                                 * Max gap/ Recess between two sessions: 6 months (article 85) meaning parliament must meet at least twice an year
                                 * Session: Period between first sitting and prorogation/dissolution
                                 * Session> Meeting> Sitting
                                 * 3 sessions annually (based on convention / admin convenience)
                                 * Budget (Feb–May) 🟰 3 months
                                 * Monsoon (July–Sep) 🟰 2 months
                                 * Winter (Nov–Dec) 🟰 1 month
                                 * Recess: Period between prorogation and next session
                                 * Adjournment: Temporarily suspends a sitting for a specified time, such as hours, days, or weeks.
                                 * Adjournment Sine Die: Ends a sitting without assigning a day for a further meeting; that is, indefinitely.
                                 * Adjournment of Debate: Postpones the discussion on a motion or resolution to a future date.
                                 * Power for adjournment  is with the Presiding Officer (Speaker/Chairman), who can recall sitting earlier if needed.
Adjournment
	Prorogation
	Terminate sitting, not session
	Session terminated
	By presiding officer
	President
	Bills/ Business of house are not affected
	Bills/ Business of house are not affected. Yet All Pending notices (except for intro of bills) lapse. 
Fresh notice has to be given for the next session.
	

                                 1. Dissolution- Only LS dissolves, 2 ways- 1. Automatic dissolution upon expiry of term 2. Dissolution by president- irrevocable
                                 1. When lok sabha dissolves, most business including bills, motions, resolutions, notices, petitions pending before it lapses. However some pending bills and all pending assurances that are to be examined by the committee on govt assurances do not lapse.
                                 2. Bills Pending in Either House: Lapse, whether originated in Lok Sabha or transmitted by Rajya Sabha.
                                 3. Bills Passed by Lok Sabha, Pending in Rajya Sabha: Lapse.
                                 4. Bills Disagreed Upon, Pending President's Notification Before Dissolution: Do not lapse.
                                 5. Bills Pending in Rajya Sabha (Not Passed by Lok Sabha): Do not lapse.
                                 6. Bills Passed by Both Houses, Pending President’s Assent: Do not lapse.
                                 7. Bills Passed by Both Houses, Returned by President for Reconsideration: Do not lapse.
                                 2. Quorum = A. 100 - 10% total members
                                 1. If no quorum, the house is either adjourned or suspended. 
                                 3. Parliament and its functioning
                                 * Voting in House
                                 * Special majority required for matters like impeachment, constitutional amendments, and removal of presiding officers.
                                 * After debate, the Speaker puts motion to voice vote: "Ayes" or "Noes." If challenged, a direct vote is conducted via slips or automatic voting.
                                 * Language in Parliament
                                 * As per Art 120 of the Indian Constitution, the business in Parliament shall be transacted in Hindi or English, but the Presiding Officer may permit a member to speak in their mother tongue.
                                 * Official Language Act, 1963 allows continued English use.
                                 * Rights of Ministers & Attorney General (Article 88)
                                 * Ministers and AGI can participate and speak in either House's proceedings as well as joint sitting without being entitled to vote. (Ministers can vote only in house of which they are a member)
                                 * Non-member ministers can participate but must get elected within 6 months.
                                 * Lame Duck Session
                                 * Last session of an outgoing Lok Sabha.
                                 * Members not re-elected are called "Lame Ducks."
Role of parliament
                                 * Legislative
                                 * Executive- control ques hour, resolutions & motions 
                                 * Fin powers & fn 
                                 * Judicial ( remove VP, judges, Prez, adjournment , motions )
                                 * Electoral
                                 * Governance - elected MP make laws
                                 * Expressive ( faith of ppl -> demo )
                                 * Highest discussion forum
                                 * Function
                                 * Select national govt
                                 * Ensures Responsible Govt- Control, guide, inform govt
                                 * Law making


Note:
                                 1. Acc to constitution, election disputes related to prez and VP- SC
                                 2. Acc to RPA, election disputes of MP, MLA, PM, CM, Speaker and Vice chairman- HC and then appeal to SC
                                 3. Salaries and allowances of MPs- determined by parliament and are provided through annual budget and are not charged on CFI. NO provision of pension for MPs. But parlia has provided.
                                 1. The salaries of speaker and Deputy speaker of LS, Chairman and Vice chairman are determined by parliament but are charged on CFI so they are not subject to annual vote of parliament.
Instruments of parliamentary control
                                 * Question hour
                                 * 1st hour of every parliamentary sitting
                                 * Mentioned in Rules of procedure
                                 * Prior notice reqd.
                                 * Speaker is final authority on admissibility
                                 * Types
                                 * Unstarred ques (white)
                                 * Written ans, no supplementary
                                 * Max 230 for a day. 
                                 * MPs can submit 10 questions, of which ask max 5. 
                                 * Starred ques (green)- StOra
                                 * Oral ans
                                 * 2 supplementary ques can be asked ( more with speaker discretion )
                                 * 15 day in advance
                                 *  only 20 ques = thru ballot
                                 * Short notice ( purple )
                                 * Oral ans
                                 * <10 day notice
                                 * Admitted at Speaker discretion
                                 * Pub imp matter
                                 * Untill 15th LS, not a single short notice question was admitted. 
                                 * Pvt ques  ( yellow )
                                 * Under rules of procedure & conduct of business
                                 * Zero hour
                                 * Indian innovation from 1962, Informal decision- Not mentioned anywhere 
                                 * Timing: Starts immediately after Question Hour in both LS and RS.
                                 * Duration: Usually lasts 30 minutes (may vary)- lasts until agenda for the day.
                                 * Purpose: MPs can raise urgent public issues without prior notice- but mins not answerable
                                 * Speaker’s/ Chairman’s  Discretion:
                                 * Half an hour
                                 * Discusses public importance issues needing clarification.
                                 * 3 days/week (as per Speaker’s discretion).
                                 * No formal motion or voting.
                                 * Short Duration Discussion (Two-Hour Discussion)
                                 * For urgent public matters (max 2 hrs).
                                 * Speaker can allot 2 days/week.
                                 * No formal motion or voting.
Motion 
	Features 
	Motions: 


	                                 * Mentioned in Rule of Procedure
                                 * No discussion on a matter of general public importance can take place except on a motion made with consent of the presiding officer.
                                 * Can be moved either by minister or private member.
3 principal categories:
                                 * Substantive -A self-contained, independent proposal dealing with important matters. Used for crucial discussions, such as confidence motions, impeachment motion.
                                 * Substitute- A motion seeking to replace the original motion with a new proposal. Alters the essence of the original motion. 
                                 * Subsidiary- A motion that has no meaning by itself but is used to aid or affect another motion. 3 subcategories: 
                                 * Ancillary Motion – Helps in routine parliamentary work (ex bill passing)
                                 * Amendment Motion – Modifies an existing motion before approval.
                                 * Superseding Motion – Replaces an ongoing debate if a new urgent issue arises.
	Closure Motion 


	                                 * A motion to conclude debate & putting matter to vote.
                                 * The Speaker/ Chairperson has discretion to accept or reject.
                                 * Types of closure motion - 
                                 * Simple Closure – Ends debate on a specific issue.
                                 * Compartmental Closure – Closes debate on different bill sections separately.
                                 * Kangaroo Closure – Speaker selects key amendments; others are ignored.
                                 * Guillotine Closure – Cuts off debate; all pending/undiscussed matters are voted on (used in budget sessions).
	Privilege Motion


	                                 * A motion raised when a member believes there is a breach of parliamentary privileges by a minister by withholding facts or giving distorted facts
                                 * Purpose- Censure the concerned minister.
                                 * Notice: Before 10 AM; discretion lies with the Speaker/Chairman. If admitted, an explanation by the Minister is required. 
                                 * Committee of Privileges
                                 * Rajya Sabha: 10 members (Deputy Chairman as Head). 
                                 * Lok Sabha: 15 members (Proportional to party strength, nominated by Speaker (ex-officio chairman).
	Calling Attention Motion
	                                 * A motion used by an MP to draw attention of the minister to an urgent matter of public importance.
                                 * An Indian innovation under Rules of Procedure.
	Adjournment Motion
	                                 * RoP of lok sabha
                                 * Purpose: Draw attention of the house towards a definite, urgent public issue linked to govt failure.
                                 * Requires 50 MPs' support to admit. Interrupts normal business.
                                 * Key Features:
                                 * Must relate to Govt conduct/ default under the Constitution & law.
                                 * The speaker has absolute discretion to refuse consent w/o giving reasons.
                                 * Lok sabha Only- Censure element, Hence, not allowed in RS.
                                 * Discussion should last no less than 2.5 hours
                                 * Restrictions:
                                 * Must be specific, factual, recent & urgent.
                                 * Single issue only (no general topics).
                                 * Cannot raise privilege questions or sub-judicial matters.
                                 * Cannot revive previous discussions in the same session.
                                 * Cannot be on a topic admissible under a separate motion.
	No confidence motion
	No-Confidence Motion (NCM):
                                 * Mentioned under Rule 198 of Lok Sabha- NOT in constitution. Although Art 75 provides for CoM as collectively responsible to LS
                                 * Does not require specific reasons.
                                 * Moved by opposition. Requires support of at least 50 MPs to introduce.
                                 * Simple majority needed to pass.
                                 * History:
                                 * First-ever motion in 1963 (against JL Nehru by J.B. Kripalani) after Sino-India War 
                                 * Indira Gandhi faced the most NCMs (15).
                                 * The 2018 motion against Narendra Modi was the 27th in India’s history.
                                 * 4 PMs have been defeated starting from morarji desai in 1979, VP Singh, Deve Gowda and Vajpayee (fell by just one vote).
	Motion of confidence
	                                 * By Council of ministers to prove its majority in LS- Only in LS. Simple majority.
                                 * If LS pass, CoM remain in power
                                 * If not pass, CoM must resign 
                                 * No specific article mentions it explicitly, but it arises from Article 75 (3) (CoM collectively responsible to Lok Sabha).
                                 * Parliamentary convention.
	Motion of thanks
	                                 * 1st session address by prez after each general election & 1st session of every fiscal year. corresponding to speech like in Britain. 
                                 * Discussed in both houses for 3 days
                                 * Amendments can be moved in form considered apt by speaker in LS, chair in RS
                                 * Must be passed, otherwise defeat of govt
                                 * Art 87, 176 mentions it
	Misc
	                                 * No day yet named motion.
                                 * Motion admitted, but no date fixed.   
                                 * Dilatory motion 
                                 * For adjournment of debate on bill, motion, resolution.
	

Censure motion
	No confidence motion
	Must State reason
	Not mandatory
	Against indiv minister/ grp/ entire council
	Entire council
	Censure CoM for specific policies
	Ascertain LS's confidence in CoM
	Need not resign
	Must resign
	                                 * Point of Order
                                 * Raised when rules/ procedures of house or consti are not followed.
                                 * Extra-ordinary device that can suspend proceedings. No debate allowed
                                 * Often used by opposition.
                                 * Special Mention of Rajya Sabha
                                 * Raises matters not covered under other motions or point of order
                                 * Equivalent to Rule 377 in Lok Sabha.
Resolution
                                 * Draw attention to gen pub interest matter
                                 * Both can be moved by any member private or minister and needs consent of the presiding officer to be admitted
                                 * All resolutions are substantive “motions”, while all motions are not substantive motions/ resolutions.
                                 * Resolution reps general will of parlia, Motion reps will of a member.
                                 * Resolution has to be written, while motion need not be written
                                 * Resolution has to be voted on while not all motions need to be voted on.
                                 * Resolutions cannot be withdrawn without leave of the house. Motion can be withdrawn with permission of the Presiding Officer. 
                                 * 3 categories- 
                                 * Pvt member resolution- discussed only on alternate friday in afternoon sitting. (twice in a month).
                                 * Committee on private members' bill & resolution
                                 * Special LS committee
                                 * 15 members
                                 * Chairman- Deputy speaker
                                 * Same fn in RS carried by business advisory committee 
                                 * Govt resolution
                                 * Moved by Minister
                                 * From Mon to Thur
                                 * Statutory resolution
                                 * Either = pvt member/ min
                                 * To pursue constitutional provisions/ parliament act
                                 * Youth Parliament- Started on recommendation of 4th All India whips conference 
                                 * Training provided by Ministry of parliamentary affairs 
Legislative Procedures- Bills
                                 * Ordinary Bill - Stages
                                 * First Reading (Introduction)
                                 * Members seek leave to introduce; if opposed, the Speaker may allow a brief statement.
                                 * Leave of the House refers to the unanimous consent of members in a legislative body to bypass standard procedures for a specific purpose.
                                 * No discussion at this stage; bill title & objectives read.
                                 * Published in Gazette of India (if published before introduction, leave not required).
                                 * Second Reading (Detailed Scrutiny & bill assumes final stage)
                                 * General Discussion: Printed copies distributed; only principles discussed, details not discussed. Any 1 of 4 actions - 
                                 * Immediately taking bill into consideration
                                 * Refer bill to select/ joint committee
                                 * Circulate bill for public opinion. 
                                 * Committee Stage:
                                 * Select/Joint Committee (ad- hoc committee): Clause-by-clause examination, amendments allowed w/o altering principle
                                 * Standing Committee: Reviews selected clauses; persuasive value.
                                 * Consideration Stage: Each clause is discussed and voted upon separately.
                                 * Third Reading (Final Vote)
                                 * Debate only on acceptance/rejection; no amendments.
                                 * Authenticated by presiding officer.
                                 * If passed, transmitted into the second House, 
                                 * Bill in Second house- which has 4 alternative:
                                 * Pass without amendments 
                                 * Return the bill- Pass with amendments (return to 1st House for reconsideration).
                                 * Reject the bill.
                                 * Keep it pending {Note- For ordinary and CAA bill- no time limit; for Money bill- 14 days only)
                                 * Deadlock-
                                 * If the 1st house rejects the amendments suggested by the 2nd house.
                                 * 2nd house rejects the bill
                                 * 2nd house keeps the bill pending for 6 months
                                 * To resolve such a deadlock, the president (under discretion) may summon a Joint sitting under article 108. 
                                 * 6 month period (for re-passing in second house) excludes prorogation, adjournment of more than 4 consecutive days.
                                 * President’s Assent under Article 111:
                                 1. Approve (Gives Assent) → Becomes an Act
                                 * Applies to all- Ordinary Bills, Article 117 Bills , CAA (Article 368), and Money Bills (110).
                                 * Note: For CAA, the President must give assent (Article 368), i.e., no veto power applies. (24th CAA)
                                 2. Withhold/Reject Assent (Absolute Veto) → Bill Fails
                                 * Applies to 2 cases
                                 * Private member bill
                                 * Applies to Ordinary Bills, Money bill and Article 117, Bills of Govt when govt resigns after passage of bills.
                                 * Ex1: In 1954, President Rajendra Prasad rejected the PEPSU Bill over concerns about improper implementation of President's Rule.
                                 * Ex2: In 1991, President R. Venkataraman withheld assent to the Salary, Allowances, and Pensions of MPs (Amendment) Bill as it was passed on the last day before Lok Sabha's dissolution without his prior consent.
                                 * NO type of veto on Money bill and CAA.
                                 3. Return (Suspensive Veto) → Parliament must reconsider
                                 * Applies only to Ordinary Bills and Article 117 Bills.
                                 * The President cannot return Money Bills (Article 110) or Constitutional Amendment Bills (Article 368).
                                 * If Parliament re-passes the bill without changes, the President must give assent.
                                 4. Pocket Veto→ Consti does not prescribe a time limit for prez to act on a bill.
                                 * The Constitution does not specify a time frame for the President to act.
                                 * Applies to Ordinary Bills and Article 117 Bills.
                                 * Example:
                                 * Indian Post Office Amendment Bill, 1986 (Zail Singh)
                                 * Money Bill (Article 110)
                                 * Article 109: special procedure for Money Bills, granting LS overriding powers, with the RS having only a recommendatory role and a 14-day limit to act.
                                 * Article 110: Defines a Money Bill & gives Criteria (Must contain at least one of these):
                                 * Tax-related: Imposition, abolition, remission, alteration, or regulation.
                                 * Union borrowing: Regulation of govt borrowing, financial obligations.
                                 * Custody & withdrawal of funds: Consolidated Fund (Art 266), Contingency Fund (Art 267).
                                 * Appropriation: Spending from the Consolidated Fund.
                                 * Declaration of expenditure charged on CFI or Increase amount of such expenditure.
                                 * Receipts of money: CFI, Public Account, custody, issue of such money. 
                                 * Audit of Union/ State accounts.
                                 * Any Incidental matters related to the above.
                                 * What is NOT a Money Bill?
                                 * Fines or penalties.
                                 * fees for licence or service charges.
                                 * Local taxes by municipal bodies.
                                 * Procedure & Speaker’s Authority
                                 * Only in LS, minister/ pvt member (Budget is only by minister), only on president’s recom; Simple majority
                                 * Speaker’s decision on whether a bill is a Money Bill is final
                                 * it can be challenged in court under judicial review if it is alleged to be unconstitutional, arbitrary, or mala fide. Ex: Aadhar case.
                                 * Sent to Rajya Sabha → Can recommend changes but cannot amend/reject → Must return within 14 days.
                                 * President's options: give Assent or withold/reject (cannot return for reconsideration).
                                 * Defeat of the money bill amounts to Govt. defeat
                                 * Financial Bills (Article 117)
                                 * Money/finance bills is a type of financial bills. But all financial bills are not money bills.  
                                 * Category A- Financial Bill (I) (Article 117) 
                                 * Contain Matters mentioned in Article 110 (Money Bill) & general legislations which are not exclusively financial in nature but are included in the bill
                                 * Procedure: Same as an Ordinary Bill but exceptions
                                 * Introduced only in LS and only on President’s recom
                                 * RS can suggest amendments or reject
                                 * President: Can assent, reject/withhold, or return
                                 * Ex- GST (Compensation to States) Bill, 2017, were considered Financial Bills under Article 117, as they included provisions beyond taxation, such as compensation mechanisms.
                                 * Category B- Financial Bill (II) 
                                 * Not a Money Bill, but Relates to Finances
                                 * Provisions Deals with expenditure from CFI but NOT matters under A. 110
                                 * Treated like an Ordinary Bill in all respects
                                 * Special feature- President’s recommendation: Not required for introduction, but a bill cannot be passed by a House unless the President has recommended its consideration in that House.
                                 * Ex- FRBM Act, 2003 – Focused on fiscal discipline without imposing new taxes or expenditures.
                                 * Joint Sitting (Article 108)
                                 * Presided by LS Speaker (Deputy Speaker in absence). RS Chairman (Vice President) cannot preside as he is not a member of either House.
                                 * Lok Sabha rules apply, not Rajya Sabha rules.
                                 * New amendments cannot be proposed except:
                                 * If they caused final disagreement.
                                 * If needed due to delay in passage.
                                 * 3 Bills Passed in Joint Sitting
                                 * Dowry Prohibition Bill (1961)
                                 * Banking Service Commission (Repeal) Bill (1978)
                                 * Prevention of Terrorism Bill (2002)
                                 * Constitutional Amendment Bill (Article 368)
                                 * Diff from ordinary bill
                                 * special majority.
                                 * No joint sitting
                                 * The President must give assent (cannot withhold or return). 
Parliamentary privileges (art 105)
                                 * Authority Over Breach- Only Parliament determines privilege breaches; courts have no jurisdiction (separation of Power)
                                 * Sources- 
                                 * Constitutional: Article 105 (Parliament) & Article 194 (State Legislatures)- expressly mentions two privileges that is freedom of speech in parliament- immunity from court proceedings and right of publication of its proceedings. 
                                 * No codification. 
                                 * Parliamentary conventions
                                 * With respect to other privileges, it provided that they were to be same as those of the british house of commons
                                 * 44th caa dropped direct reference to the house of commons in consti and said it will stay the same as in 1979.
                                 * Laws - 
                                 * Parlia proceedings (protection of publication) act 1997 protect press' rights
                                 * Substantially true report
                                 * No malice, pub good
                                 * Not secret house meeting
                                 * Code of Civil Procedure (CPC) - protection from arrest/detention.  
                                 * Rules of LS and RS-  LS Speaker/ RS Chairman must be notified of a member's arrest.
                                 * Judicial interpretations- 
                                 * Privileges cannot override constitutional rights (keshav Singh v. Speaker case 1965- Parliament can punish individuals- reprimand, arrest for breach of parlia privileges as long as they don't diminish their FRs)
                                 * Journalists need wider immunity for reporting proceedings & RT privacy cant prevent publication of public records {R. Rajagopal v. State of Tamil Nadu (1994)}.
                                 * Eligible for- RS and LS MPs, Parlia committees, Union Ministers, AGI
                                 * Types- 
                                 * Individual:
                                 * Freedom of speech in the House. A. 105)
                                 * Protection of members from civil arrest during sessions, Freedom from arrest under preventive detention laws
                                 * Exemption from jury duty (appear as jury in a case. No more applicable in india) 
                                 * Collective:
                                 * Parliament have the authority to control their own proceedings, ensuring orderly conduct.
                                 * Parliament or its committees can call individuals (including officials & pvt citizens) to provide information, give testimony, or assist in investigations.
                                 * What constitutes breach ?
                                 * No clear codification. Various acts obstruct proceeding consists  
                                 * Outsider disturbance
                                 * Member assaulted
                                 * Speech/ write abt char of legislator 
                                 * Press Not reporting parliamentary proceedings accurately and responsibly
                                 * Significance
                                 * Legislative Independence: MPs have immunity from court proceedings, allowing them to debate policies freely without legal pressure. (E.g., JMM Bribery Case (1998)—MPs from Narsimha rao govt were granted immunity for votes despite bribery charges.)
                                 * Accountability & Transparency: The right to publish proceedings keeps the public informed. (E.g., Live telecast of Parliament debates began in 1989, enhancing public awareness. Later, Lok Sabha TV and Rajya Sabha TV expanded coverage, which merged into Sansad TV in 2021.)
                                 * Prevention of Executive Overreach: Article 105 ensures MPs' freedom of speech. (E.g., Somnath Chatterjee (2008) refused to resign as Lok Sabha Speaker despite party pressure.)
                                 * Ensuring Ethical Conduct: E.g., Cash-for-Query Scam (2005) – MPs were caught accepting bribes to ask questions in Parliament and were expelled, showing accountability for misconduct.
                                 * Limitations to parliamentary privileges
                                 * Free Speech reasonable Restrictions (Article 19) – Ex: Expulsion of MP Ramesh Bidhuri (2023) – for making communal remarks against a Muslim MP in Parliament, proving that Article 105 does not protect hate speech or misconduct.
                                 * Judicial Review (Article 21) – E.g., Keshav Singh Case – UP Legislative Assembly punished Keshav Singh for contempt, but the Supreme Court ruled that judicial review applies even to legislative privileges if its ultra vires FR.
                                 * No Criminal immunity – E.g., Lalu Prasad Yadav Fodder Scam – Convicted for corruption despite being an MP, proving that parliamentary privileges do not protect against criminal prosecution.
                                 * Challenges
                                 * Violates the principle of natural justice – MPs judge their own cases.
                                 * Excessive legislative control– Risks privilege misuse. Ex: Keshav singh case
                                 * Way Forward
                                 * Clear Codification – Example: UK’s Parliamentary Privileges Act, 1987 – Clearly outlines parliamentary privileges and limits their misuse, ensuring accountability while preserving legislative freedom.
                                 * Judicial Oversight – Ensure privileges do not violate fundamental rights. Ex: Keshav singh case, SC reiterated the need for checks on privilege misuse
                                 * Ethical Reforms & Accountability – Ex: Nolan Committee (UK, 1994) – Established ethical standards for public officials, including MPs.
	

 Budget in India
Category
	Details
	Article 112
	Refers to Annual Financial Statement (AFS), not explicitly "Budget."- presented on such a day as the President may direct.


Functions of Budget (Make cloud diagram)
                                 1. Instrument of control- Ex: imposing Sin Tax on tobacco and alcohol
                                 2. Harbinger of Change- Ex: 15 FC performance based grants, PLI, Gender budgeting
                                 3. Economic growth- Ex: Recent tax slab restructuring (0 tax till ₹12 lakh) stimulating demand; reducing corporation tax
                                 4. Transparency and Public accountability- Ex: Outcome based budgeting
	Types of Budgets
	                                 * Full Budget: Govt's estimate for the entire FY. 
                                 *  Interim Budget
                                 * A complete budget (includes revenue & expenditure) presented in an election year. Valid for a few months until the new government presents a full budget.
                                 * Not mandatory but a convention for continuity
	Vote on Account
	                                 * Grants approval for govt expenditure only for a short period (usually 2 months (~1/6th of annual estimation).
                                 * No new policies/ taxes or major changes are introduced.
                                 * Ensures smooth functioning until the full budget is passed
	Vote of Credit 
	                                 * Granted for unexpected or emergency expenditures, like war or natural disasters. Considered a blank cheque for urgent national needs.
                                 * Allows the govt to spend beyond the approved budget without detailed breakdowns.
	Prepared by?
	Department of Economic Affairs (DEA) & Finance Ministry (FM).
	Components of Budget
	Actuals → For the preceding FY (2023-24), based on finalized accounts
Revised Estimates (RE) → For current financial year (2024-25), updating previous BE based on actual spending trends.
Budget Estimates (BE) → For next financial year (2025-26).
	Consolidated Fund of India (Art 266)
	                                 * Includes all govt revenues (e.g., taxes, service receipts) and loans raised. 
                                 * Covers all govt spending, including debt repayment & loans to states/UTs. 
                                 * Requires parliamentary authorization.
	Contingency Fund of India (Art 267)
	                                 * Imprest/ advanced fund at the disposal of the President for unforeseen expenses. Used for emergencies (e.g., natural calamities). 
                                 * Replenished from the CFI periodically with parliamentary approval.
	Public Account of India
	                                 * Money received by the govt other than the Consolidated Fund (e.g., provident funds, small savings, remittance i.e. internal govt transfer). 
                                 * Govt’s Role: Acts as a banker/ trustee, refunding money after contract completion.
                                 * Parliamentary Approval: Not required b/c it doesn't belong to Govt & have to be paid back to persons & authorities who deposited into it. 
	FRBM Act – Policy Targets
	1. FRBM Act & 2018 Amendments:
                                 * Debt-to-GDP Target: 60% by FY 2024-25 (Centre: 40%, States: 20%).
                                 * Fiscal Deficit Target: 3% of GDP (Union) & 3% of GSDP (States).
                                 * Revenue Deficit or Effective Revenue deficit: No longer a mandatory target.
                                 * Escape Clause: Allows 0.5% fiscal slippage for national security, natural calamities, economic crises, structural reforms, and agricultural declines.
2. Budget 2025 Fiscal Indicators:
                                 * Fiscal Deficit: 4.4% of GDP (2025-26), down from 4.8% (2024-25). Declining since last 3 year
                                 * Revenue Deficit: 1.5% of GDP (2025-26), down from 1.9% (2024-25). Declining since last 3 years. 
                                 * Debt-to-GDP Ratio:  70% (2018)- increased to 83% (2024) (Centre: 57%, States: 26%)-  Debt Reduction Target: Centre aims to lower debt to 50% of GDP by March 2031.
	Elements of the Budget
	                                 * Estimates: Revenue and capital receipts. 
                                 * Revenue Augmentation: ways and means to raise revenue.
                                 * Estimates: Revenue and capital expenditure
                                 * Actuals: Details of actual receipts and exp and reasons of deficit or surplus of closing year 
                                 * Economic and Fiscal Policy: Outline of policies for the upcoming year like Taxes and schemes.
	 
	                                 * Acworth Committee (1921): Recommended separation of railway finances from general finances. 
                                 * Merger in 2016: Railway and general budgets merged for streamlining.
	Constitutional Provisions Related to the Budget
	Article 112: President must lay before Parliament (LS and RS) a statement of estimated receipts & expenditures. 
Appropriation bill/ Parliamentary Authority (Art 114): Money can be withdrawn only under parliamentary appropriation. 
Finance/ Money Bill (110): Cannot be introduced without the President’s recommendation.
Role of Houses: Money bills originate in Lok Sabha; Rajya Sabha can suggest amendments but not vote on grants.
	Charged Exp (Article 113)
	Expenditures like interest payments, salaries of constitutional officeholders, etc. Not subject to a vote but can be discussed in Parliament.
                                 * 2nd schedule- Emoluments & Allowances of Key Constitutional Authorities- (President, Chairman and VC of RS, Speaker and deputy S of LS, Judges of SC and HC, CAG)
                                 * Salary, Allowances, admin charges and Pensions of UPSC & ECI.
                                 * Debt related liabilities, Interest payment and repayment of Debt - both internal & external. 
                                 * Other constitutional liabilities- Any sums required to satisfy judgments, decrees, or awards of courts/tribunals; Pension of past Governors and other similar liabilities
                                 * ​The Governor and HC judges draws a salary from the Consolidated fund of the state but the pension from the Consolidated fund of India.
	

                                 1. Order of Precedence:
                                 * Not explicitly mentioned in the Indian Constitution itself but is established by executive orders of the President of India For ceremonial purposes only (determined by the President Secretariat, maintained by MHA).
                                 * First 5 Ranks: Formal Executives of the Union and States, except Rank 3 (PM).
                                 * President
                                 * Vice-President
                                 * Prime Minister
                                 * Governors of States within their respective States
                                 * Former Presidents
                                 * Rank 5A: Deputy Prime Minister.
                                 * Rank 6- CJI and LS Speaker
                                 * Rank 7: Union Ministers, LoP, former PMs, CMs, and Vice Chair of NITI Aayog.
                                 * Rank 7A: Bharat Ratna
                                 * Rank 8: Ambassadors Extraordinary and Plenipotentiary and High Commissioners of Commonwealth countries accredited to India
Governors of States (outside their respective states)
Chief Ministers of States (within their respective states)
                                 * Rank 9- Judges of the Supreme Court
                                 * Rank 9A- Chairman of the Union Public Service Commission
Chief Election Commissioner of India
Comptroller and Auditor General of India
                                 * Rank 14- Chief justice of HC
                                    2. Cabinet Committees:
                                    * Accommodation: Headed by the Home Minister.
                                    * Parliamentary Affairs: Headed by the Defence Minister.
RPA 1950- seats, Electoral roll, Voters (regis, qual, disqual), Parties (regis), ECI- SVEEP
Provision
	Detailed Explanation
	Part I: Allocation of Seats
	- Defines the allocation of seats in the LS and SLA based on the population. 
- This ensures that each state gets a proportionate number of seats. 
- The allocation is subject to periodic revisions based on population changes.
	Part II: Registration of Electors & electoral rolls
	- Defines the process for compiling and maintaining electoral rolls. 
- Electoral rolls are updated regularly. 
- It includes the creation of a permanent list of voters and a process for updating it based on changes in residency, age, etc.
	Part III: Qualification of Voters
	- Specifies that only Indian citizens, aged 18 years or more, residing in the constituency are eligible to be registered as voters. 
- Provides special conditions for people living in certain areas (ex migrants).
	Part IV: Disqualification of Voters
	- Voters can be disqualified for reasons such as being of unsound mind or being convicted of certain criminal offenses. 
- Specific disqualifications apply in cases of disloyalty or violation of laws related to elections.
	Part V: Preparation of Electoral Rolls
	

	Part VI: Registration of Political Parties
	- Sets out the rules for the registration of political parties with the ECI. 
- Political parties need to be registered to participate in elections.
	Part VII: Administration and Control of Elections
	- The Election Commission of India is estd as the authority responsible for conducting elections at the national and state levels. 
- Provides provisions for the powers, duties, and structure of the Election Commission in overseeing the election process.
	RPA 1950- seats, Electoral roll, Voters (regis, qual, disqual), Parties (regis), ECI- SVEEP
Representation of the People Act, 1951 (RPA 1951)
Provision
	Detailed Explanation
	Part I: General Provisions
	- Deals with the conduct of elections to the LS and SLA. 
- Defines the election procedure, election of representatives by direct voting. 
- Addresses various aspects of election machinery and procedure, including the role of the Election Commission.
	Part II: Qual and Disqual of Candidates
	- Specifies that candidates must be Indian citizens, at least 25 years old for the Lok Sabha and 30 years for the Rajya Sabha. (Also in consti- art 84)
- Disqualifies candidates found guilty of criminal offenses (e.g., treason, bribery, etc.), or those with debts beyond a certain limit, from contesting elections.
- A person confined in prison, whether under a sentence of imprisonment or transportation or otherwise, or is in the lawful custody of the police can not vote. 
	Part III: Election Petitions
	- Allows for filing election petitions in case of disputes regarding election results. 
- It provides grounds for challenging elections such as corrupt practices, improper conduct, or illegal practices during elections. 
- Specifies the process to file petitions, time limits, and resolution mechanisms.
- HC has original jurisdiction over election petitions for parliament & SLA elections. 
- Ground to declare election Void - 
                                    * Improper rejection of nomination
                                    * Corrupt practices (by candidate/agent/supporters)
                                    * Constitutional non-compliance
	Part IV: Conduct of Elections
	- Lays down the rules for conducting elections, including the election process, poll stations, and the voting process. 
- It includes provisions for the use of (EVMs) in elections, and the proper procedures for vote counting, announcement of results, and declaration of winners.
	Part V: Corrupt Practices
	- Defines various corrupt practices during elections such as bribery, undue influence, or offering inducements to voters. 
- Specifies that anyone found guilty of corrupt practices may face disqualification and legal punishment, including imprisonment or fines.
	Part VI: Illegal Practices
	- Deals with illegal practices like impersonating voters or voting multiple times. 
- Specifies penalties for these offenses, including imprisonment or disqualification from future elections.
	Part VII: Offences and Penalties
	- Lists all offenses related to elections such as bribery, undue influence, or false declarations. 
- Specifies penalties for these offenses, including fines, disqualification from elections, or imprisonment. The aim is to maintain the integrity of the election process.
	Part VIII: Voting Rights of Certain Persons
	- Specifies that certain persons like government employees, members of the armed forces, and citizens living abroad may face restrictions or special procedures for voting, such as absentee voting or postal ballots.
  

	Part IX: Appointment of Election Officials
	- Provides for the appointment of various election officials like returning officers, presiding officers, and other election staff responsible for conducting the election process. 
	

Key Differences Between RPA 1950 and RPA 1951:
Aspect
	RPA 1950
	RPA 1951
	Focus
	Primarily focuses on the registration of voters and preparation of electoral rolls.
	Deals with the conduct of elections, including qual and disqual for candidates, voting, election petitions, disputes, corruption.
	Voter/ candidate
	Deals with “voters” qualifications.
	Specifies eligibility and disqualification of “candidates”.
	Election Disputes
	No 
	yes- election petitions and disputes wrt election results.
	Corrupt Practices
	No
	Defines corrupt practices like bribery, undue influence, and their penalties.
	

Parliamentary committees 


Parliamentary Committees in India are not explicitly detailed in the Constitution but derive authority from Articles 105 (Parliamentary privileges) and article 118 (Rule of procedure), while the term "committee" appears in various provisions without specifying composition, tenure, or functions. 
4 fold Criteria- 
                                    * Appointed  or elected by house or speaker/ Chairman 
                                    * Works under direction of speaker/ Chairman 
                                    * Executive Body – Appointed by Parliament, reports to Speaker/Chairman.
                                    * Secretariat Support – Assisted by Lok Sabha & Rajya Sabha.
                                    * Consultative Committees which include MPs are not Parliamentary Committees as they lack parliamentary oversight powers and don't fulfil above 4 conditions.
                                    * Co-terminus with LS. 
                                    * 10-30 members, 
                                    * Ministers are chairpersons; Constituted under Min of Parliamentary Affairs
Classification
                                    * Standing Committees: These are permanent committees that work continuously to examine bills, policies, and administration, such as the PAC, 24 DRSCs.
                                    * Ad hoc Committees: These are temporary committees formed for a specific purpose and dissolved after completing their task, such as the Inquiry committees and Joint Parliamentary Committee (JPC) on Stock Market Scam (2001).
Financial Committees
                                    * Public Accounts Committee (PAC)
                                    * Estd: 1921 under GoI Act 1919.
                                    * Composition: 22 members (15 LS, 7 RS);  elected annually via proportional representation.
                                    * Chairmanship: From opposition, appointed by Speaker as per convention
                                    * Restriction: Ministers not included.
                                    * Committee is assisted by CAG- acts as guide, friend, philosopher of PAC. 
                                    * Functions
                                    * Examines CAG reports laid by Prez – Appropriation account and finance accounts of Union govt + Audits state corporations, trading, manufacturing projects (except those under Public Undertakings Committee) + autonomous, semi-autonomous body accounts.
                                    * Checks legal & technical compliance – Ensures spending aligns with Appropriation Act. Ex: Is the money legally available? Does it conform to authority that governs it and the rules?
                                    * Reviews public expenditure –from PoV economy, prudence, wisdom, proprietary
                                    * Scrutinizes excess Grants before lok sabha votes.
                                    * Limitations
                                    * Policy-neutral – not concerned with policy in a broader sense.
                                    * Post-mortem financial review- after exp has been incurred. 
                                    * Advisory & Non-binding recommendations
                                    * No role in day-to-day administration.
                                    * Estimates Committee
                                    * Origin in Standing finance committee in 1921. First estimate committee post independence was formed in 1950 by then finance minister John mathai
                                    * Members –30 members of LS ONLY
                                    * Elected annually via proportional representation, Ministers not eligible.
                                    * Term – 1 year 
                                    * Chairman appointed by the speaker from the ruling party as per convention. 
                                    * Functions 
                                    * Continuous economic committee - examines estimates included in the budget after voting and suggests economies in public expenditure. 
                                    * Examine every year selected ministries and departments. Covering all of them in years, suggesting alternative policies to bring efficiency.
                                    * Examine if money was well laid within policy limits
                                    * Suggest how an estimate to be presented in parliament , but no function already undertaken by the Public undertaking committee.
                                    * Limitations
                                    * Only examine budget estimate after voting, not before
                                    * Can’t question policy laid by parliament.
                                    * Advisory, Recommendation not binding.
                                    * Lacks expert assistance of CAG
                                    * Work nature of post mortem.
                                    * Committee on Public Undertakings (PSUs)
                                    * Created: 1964 (Krishna Menon Committee recommendation).
                                    * Members: 15 (initial), 22  since (1974) – 15 LS, 7 RS.
                                    * Term: 1 year.
                                    * Election: By Parliament (Proportional Representation), Ministers not elected.
                                    * Chairman: Appointed by Speaker (LS only).
                                    * Functions:
                                    * Examines reports, accounts, and CAG reports on public undertakings.
                                    * Evaluates fund management as per sound business principles.
                                    * Exercises functions of PAC & Estimates Committee on public undertakings (as allotted by Speaker).
                                    * Limitations:
                                    * Can't examine: 
                                    * matters distinct from business or commercial functions of PSU 
                                    * Daily administrative matters of Pub undertakings
                                    * More than 10-12 undertakings.
                                    * Post-mortem in nature.
                                    * Advisory, not binding.
Departmental standing committee 
                                    * Setup: Rule Committee recommendation for financial accountability.
                                    * Structure: 24 Committees (8 RS, 16 LS); 31 members (21 LS, 10 RS).
                                    * Appointment: LS members nominated by Speaker, RS by Chairman. 
                                    * Ministers ineligible.
                                    * Term: 1 year.
                                    * Functions:
                                    * Ensure financial accountability of executive to legislature
                                    * Examine Demands for Grants before LS discussion. Must not recommend cut motions.
                                    * Scrutinize bills and annual reports of the concerned ministry.
                                    * Review long-term national policy documents.
                                    * Limitations:
                                    * Can't examine administration matters.
                                    * Can't review issues considered by other committees.
                                    * Advisory in nature.
                                    * Procedure:
                                    * Demands for Grants: House adjourns post-budget discussion → Review → Report submitted (no extension) → House considers.
                                    * Bills: Examines principles, clauses → Reports within time.
                                    * Separate report for each ministry. 
                                    * Merits:
                                    * Non-partisan proceedings.
                                    * More flexible than LS.
                                    * Strengthens parliamentary control.
                                    * Ensures efficiency & economy in public spending.
                                    * Enables MPs to engage & understand governance.
                                    * Experts can be invited for insights.
FYI - 
                                    * All committees under RS- HECH PEST (HOME affairs, edu, commerce & industry, Health, personal law & justice, Env, SnT, Transport, tourism & Culture )
                                    * Committees under LS - Defense, Energy, External affairs, FInance, IT, agri etc.
Committees to Inquire (PPE )
                                    * Committee on Petitions: Examines petitions on bills, public matters, and representations from individuals/associations (Union subjects). (15 LS, 10 RS)
                                    * Committee of Privileges: Investigates breaches of parliamentary privileges; semi-judicial authority (15 LS, 10 RS)
                                    * Ethics Committee: Enforces MPs' code of conduct; recommends disciplinary actions. (Varies in LS & RS)
                                    * In RS - 1997 ; LS - 2000
                                    * The Ethics Committee can take up only cases of misconduct that involve MP.
Committees to Scrutinize & Control:
