                                    * Govt. Assurances: Monitors ministers' promises on floor of house and ensures timely implementation. (15 LS, 10 RS)
                                    * Papers Laid on Table: Scrutinizes documents presented in the House for constitutional compliance. (15 LS, 10 RS)
                                    * Subordinate Legislation: Reviews executive’s rule-making powers under parliamentary delegation. ( 15 LS, 15 RS )
                                    * SC/ST Welfare: Examines NC-SC/ST reports; oversees community welfare measures. (20 LS, 10 RS) 
                                    * Minister is NOT eligible for election 
                                    * Women’s Empowerment: Reviews NCW reports; assesses govt. efforts on women's dignity, equality. (30 members: 20 LS, 10 RS)
                                    * Joint committee on office of profit - 15 members (10 LS , 5 RS)- Examines govt-appointed bodies and recommends if office-holders should be disqualified based on OoP.  (OoP is decided by Prez in consultation with ECI)
Committees Relating to Day-to-Day Business of the House
                                    * Business Advisory Committee (BAC)
                                    * Function: Regulates House schedule, allocates time for bills and business.
                                    * Composition: 15 Lok Sabha members (Speaker as Chairman); 11 Rajya Sabha members (RS Chairman as ex-officio Chairman).
                                    * Constitution: Formed at Lok Sabha's commencement and reconstituted as needed.
                                    * Term: No fixed term; members serve until replaced.
                                    * Membership: Nominated by the Speaker. .
                                    * Rules Committee
                                    * Function: Recommends amendments to House procedures and rules.
                                    * Chairman - LS Speaker & RS chairman in both
                                    *  LS & RS 
                                    * Committee on pvt members bill= Only in LS; Chairman- Depy Speaker 
                                    * Committee on absence of members- Only in LS (in RS, such matter dealt by house itself) 
Housekeeping committees
                                    * General Purposes Committee
                                    * Separate in both LS & RS. 
                                    * Composition: Speaker, Deputy Speaker, panel of chairpersons & panel of vice chairperson in RS. chairman of standing committees, leaders of recognized parties, and Speaker-nominated members.
                                    * Chairmanship: Speaker is ex-officio Chairman.
                                    * Function: Advises on House affairs as referred by the Speaker.
                                    * House Committee- 
                                    * Function: Manages MPs’ residential accommodation & oversees related facilities.
                                    * Joint Committee on Salaries and Allowances of MPs
                                    * Legal Basis: Salary, Allowances, and Pension of MPs Act, 1954.
                                    * Composition: 10 Lok Sabha members (nominated by Speaker), 5 Rajya Sabha members (nominated by Chairman).
                                    * Function: Regulates salaries and amenities (housing, telephone, postal, secretarial, medical).
Cabinet Committee on Security (CCS)
                                    * Function: Handles key decisions on national security, defence policy, major appointments, defence expenditure.
                                    * Composition: Chaired by PM; includes MEA, Home, Finance, and Defence.
                                    * Implementation Bodies:
                                    * Defence Procurement Board – Headed by Defence Secretary.
                                    * Defence Production Board – Headed by Secretary (Defence Production).
                                    * Defence R&D Board – Headed by Secretary (Defence R&D).
State legislature
Category
	Details
	Constitutional Provisions
	Part 6
Articles 153-167: Related to State Executive.
Article 168 states that every state shall have a Legislature, which may be unicameral or bicameral 
	State Executive
	Governor, Chief Minister, Council of Ministers, Advocate General of the State.
	Composition
	Same as Parliament (directly elected on universal adult franchise).
	Strength of Legislative Assembly
	Strength not prescribed. (Maximum: 500, Minimum: 60).
Exceptions due to low population: Arunachal Pradesh(30), Sikkim (32), Goa (40), Mizoram (40), Nagaland (46).
	Largest SLA
	UP (403) > West Bengal (294) > Maharashtra (288) > Tamil Nadu (234). (UPWe MaTa)
	Nominated Members 
	(no longer applicable).
	Territorial Constituencies
	Same as Parliament (states divided into territorial constituencies on ratio “population:seats” for equal and uniform representation)- Article 170
	Delimitation Freeze
	Same as Parliament:
- 42nd Amendment Act, 1976: Froze total seats and division of constituencies at 1971 levels till 2000.
- 84th Amendment Act, 2001: Extended freeze till 2026, 1991 Census.
- 87th Amendment Act, 2003:  2001 Census.
	Seat Reservation
	Same as Parliament (SC/ST reservation based on population).
	Art 169
	- Can be created/abolished by Parliament by simple majority if the State Assembly passes a resolution by special majority
	Strength of SLC (Vidhan parishad)
	Unlike Parliament ( Min: 40, Max: 1/3rd of Assembly ). But actual strength is fixed by parliament. Size of SLC depends upon size of SLA. 
	Manner of Election to Legislative Council
	Unlike Parliament:
                                    * - ⅓ elected by MLAs.
                                    * - ⅓ elected by local bodies (e.g., municipalities, panchayats).
                                    * - remaining ⅓ 
                                    * 1/12 elected by graduates (3 years standing).
                                    * 1/12 elected by teachers (3 years standing, not below secondary level).
                                    * - 1/6th Remainder nominated by Governor (experts in arts, science, literature, social service, cooperative movement)- The bonafide or propriety of governor’s nomination cannot be challenged in courts in any case.
                                    * PR-STV
	States with a Bicameral Legislature
	No uniformity in organisation of state legislature. 22 states have unicameral legislature
MAKTUB- TN abolished bicameralism in 1986, revived in 2010, but was put on hold.
	Duration of SLA
	Same as Parliament (5-year term, extendable during National Emergency, re-election within 6 months after emergency revocation).
	Duration of SLC
	Same as Rajya Sabha (Permanent body, ⅓ members retire every 2 years).
	Membership Qualifications
	Same as Parliament (25 years for Assembly, 30 years for Council).
RPA act 1951- 
                                    * elected MLA/ MLC must be an elector in the concerned state 
                                    * nominated MLA/ MLC only needs to be a resident, not an elector. 
                                    * elected MPs must  be an elector in any LS/ VS constituency in India 
                                    * nominated MPs need not be resident in any state or elector in any constituency
	Disqualification Criteria
	Same as Parliament (Office of Profit, election offenses, corruption, etc.).
The Governor decides disqualification in consultation with the ECI (unlike the President at Centre).
	Defection
	Same as Parliament (as per Tenth Schedule)
	Presiding Officers SLA
	Same as Parliament (Speaker and Deputy Speaker elected by Assembly, 14-day notice required for removal).
	Presiding Officers (SLC)
	Unlike Parliament (RS):
- Only SLC elects Chairman & Deputy Chairman.
- Chairman is a member of State legis.
	Language of State Legislature
	Same as Parliament (English is the official language, states can adopt regional languages).
	Salaries
	Salaries and allowances of speaker and deputy speaker, chairman and deputy chairman is charged on the consolidated fund of state.
	Ordinary bill process
	SLC is a secondary house
Parliament
	State Legislature
	1. It can be introduced in either House of Parliament.
	Same as Parliament
	2. It can be introduced either by a minister or by a private member.
	Same as Parliament
	3. It passes through first reading, second reading, and third reading in the originating House.
	Same as Parliament
	4. It is deemed to have been passed only when both Houses agree, either with or without amendments.
	Same as Parliament
	5. A deadlock occurs if the second House rejects the bill, proposes unacceptable amendments, or does not pass it within six months.
	Deadlock occurs if the Legislative Council does not pass the bill within three months.
	6. A joint sitting of both Houses is convened to resolve a deadlock.
	No provision for joint sitting.
	7. Lok Sabha cannot override Rajya Sabha; only a joint sitting can resolve a deadlock.
	The Legislative Assembly can override the Legislative Council by passing the bill for the second time. If rejected again or delayed beyond one month, the bill is deemed passed in the form passed by the Assembly. (So, SLC can delay/detain the bill at most for 4 month)
	8. The joint sitting applies to a bill originating in either House.
	Second-time passage applies only to bills originating in the Assembly. If the Assembly rejects a bill from the Council, it is dead.
	

	Money Bill Process
	Exact same provisions as Parliament
Parliament
	State Legislature
	1. introduced only in the LS.
	Same as Parliament (Only in SLA).
	2. It requires the President's recommendation.
	Requires the Governor’s recommendation.
	3. It can be introduced only by a minister
	Same as Parliament
	4. RS cannot reject or amend it; must return within 14 days with or without recommendations.
	Same as Parliament 
	5. If LS accepts a recommendation, the bill is deemed passed in modified form.
	Same as Parliament
	6. If LS rejects all recommendations, the bill is deemed passed in its original form.
	Same as Parliament
	7. If Rajya Sabha does not return the bill in 14 days, it is deemed passed as originally approved by Lok Sabha.
	Same as Parliament .
	. No resolution mechanism for deadlock; Lok Sabha's decision prevails.
	Same as Parliament 
	

	Governor’s Assent
	Governor has 4 options:
                                    * Give assent (bill becomes law).
                                    * Withhold assent (bill ends).
                                    * Return for reconsideration (must assent if passed again – Suspensive Veto)
                                    * Reserve for President’s consideration. (Article 200)
                                    * Mandatory
                                    * If it lowers the position/ powers of HC
                                    * Governor’s discretion 
                                    * Against the provisions of the Constitution (Ex: FRs, federalism)
                                    * Deals with compulsory acquisition of property under Article 31A
                                    * If the bill affects the larger national interest
                                    * If it is of grave national importance
                                    * If it contradicts Union policies ex: DPSPs
	President’s Assent
	Returned bills must be reconsidered within 6 months, the President not bound to give assent even for the second time.
	

Position of Legislative Council
	SLC is established under Article 168 of the Indian Constitution, and its composition, creation, and abolition are governed by Articles 169, 171, and 172.
	Equal Powers with Assembly (Importance)
	                                    * Introduction and passage of ordinary bills -Prevents hasty, defective, ill-considered legislation as it can detain legislations upto 4 months
                                    * Approval of governor ordinances.
                                    * Consideration of reports of state finance commission, CAG, SPSC
                                    * Enlargement of jurisdiction of SPSC.
                                    * Selection of ministers and CM- they can be members of either house. Allows representation of professionals and experts who may not contest elections.
	Unequal Powers with Assembly (criticism)
	

                                    * Final power of passing of Ordinary bill = In case of disagreement between SLA and SLC, will of SLA prevails it can only delay up to 4 months. So in other words, it is not even a revising body like Rajya sabha. It is only a dilatory or advisory body.
                                    * Money Bill
                                    * Ratification of CAA- Will of SLA prevails
                                    * Does not participate in elections of President or Rajya sabha. 
                                    * Art 169- Very existence of SLC depends upon SLA.
Secondary chamber/ costly ornamental luxury- SLC is a subordinate house in all respects. Seen as a refuge for defeated politicians. Enables unpopular or rejected politicians to become CM or Ministers
	Why has Rajya sabha been accorded more importance than SLC?
	                                    1. Federalism- Rajya Sabha represents states. Therefore, Rajya Sabha is an effective revising body, Council is only advisory. 
                                    2. Homogeneity of Rajya sabha- SLC has Heterogeneous composition (elected + nominated members) weakens authority
                                    3. Democratic Justification=  SLC must yield to the Assembly (directly elected house).
	Chief Secretary
	                                    * Appointed by CM.
                                    * No fixed tenure (unlike Central Cabinet Secretary – 2 years). Can be extended
                                    * A non-constitutional post created to assist State Government like the Central secretariat, with ministers handling the department politically and secretaries managing administration.
                                    * Functions of Chief Secretary
                                    * General Functions- Interdepartmental coordination, progress monitoring of schemes. 
                                    * Service-Related Functions
                                    * Appoints officers in State services, imposes major punishments
                                    * Handles senior appointments, promotions, transfers, and disciplinary actions against gazetted officers.
                                    * Note Cabinet secretary dont exercise such admin functions
	President  (Art 52-78) 
                                    * Union exec composed of 
                                    * President
                                    * VP( 63) - american model
                                    * PM and his CoM
                                    * Attorney General
                                    * Article 52 – There shall be a President of India. (British model)
                                    * Article 53 – The executive power of the Union shall be vested in the President and exercised by them directly or through subordinate officers as per the Constitution.
                                    * Prez is an integral/ constituent part of Parliament ( as bill needs his consent ), though he's not a member of either house.
                                    * Neelam Sanjeeva Reddy:
                                    * Became President in 1977.
                                    * Only unopposed/unanimous President of India.
                                    * Never served as Vice President.
                                    * Qualification for elections
                                    * Indian citizen of at least 35 years, naturalised citizen can become too 
                                    * Must qualify to become a member of LOK SABHA
                                    * Should not hold any office of profit
                                    * Subscribed by 50 electors as proposers, 50- seconders. 
                                    * ₹15k deposit- RBI (not parliament)
                                    * Article 54- election process
                                    * Who elects?
                                    * Only Elected members ALLOWED  -
                                    * both Parliament houses
                                    * SLA
                                    * Legislative assembly- UT of DL, Puducherry,J&K
                                    * Nominated members and SLC NOT allowed in election
                                    * If the Assembly dissolved? Doesn’t matter
                                    * Vote values
                                    * Value of MLA Votes: Depends on state population (1971 Census). Total population divided by no of MLA of state
                                    * Value of MP Votes: Total value of MLA votes divided by the number of MPs.
                                    * Voting Process: Secret ballot with STV-PR.    
                                       * Election process of President = Proportional Representation by Single Transferable Vote (STV) – Ensuring Absolute Majority
                                       * Types of Proportional Representation:
                                       * STV (India follows RS, SLC, President, VP).
                                       * List System (Used in some countries,).
                                       * PR  elects multiple MPs per constituency
                                       * Voting Process:
                                       * Voters mark preferences on one ballot.
                                       * Phase 1: Count first preference votes. If quota met → elected.
                                       * If no majority: Least vote-getter eliminated, votes transferred based on next preference.
                                       * Process repeats until a candidate wins.
                                       * Nature of Election: Indirect
                                       * Aligns with the parliamentary system- nominal head.
                                       * Saves time & energy.
                                       * Direct election could lead to single-party dominance.
                                       * For Presidential Election: Preferential Vote System is better as it’s a single-seat election.
                                       * Article 56- prez continues to hold office until successor is elected, preventing interregnum. However NO SUCH CONSTITUTIONAL PROVISION FOR VP ( though he does hold office) 
                                       * Article 60- Oath of prez - preserve, defend, protect (UDP) constn + Service and well being of Indian people
                                       * VP (art 63)= true faith/ allegiance -> constn
                                       * Art 61- For impeachment
                                       * Both elected and nominated members of parliament.
                                       * quasi judicial procedure
                                       * grounds = violation of constitution (undefined)
                                       * Charge initiation = 1/4 member either house,
                                       * After 14 day notice 
                                       * Majority = 2/3rd of total membership of house
                                       * Then passed to other house
                                       * Prez = RT appear, be represented.
                                       * Election disputes= SC (A 71)
                                       * ONLY auth to decide
                                       * Petition filed only by presidential candidate
                                       * ≥20 elector = joined petitioner within 30 days
                                       * If election void by SC - acts by president not invalidated
                                       * SLA dissolution is not ground to prevent holding election of prez
                                       * Article 72- Grant of Pardons, reprieves, respites or remissions of punishment or to suspend, remit or commute the sentence of any person convicted of any offence under—
                                       * Court Martial;
                                       * For an offence against any law relating to a matter to which the executive power of the Union extends;
                                       * Where the sentence is a sentence of death.
                                       * President & governor have concurrent jurisdiction for suspension, remission, and commutation of death sentence.
                                       * Types -
                                       * Pardon – Completely removes punishment
                                       * Commutation – Reduces punishment to a lighter form (e.g., death → life imprisonment). CoLi
                                       * Remission – Shortens the sentence duration but keeps the nature of punishment unchanged. RemDura
                                       * Respite – Lesser sentence due to special circumstances (e.g., pregnancy).
                                       * Reprieve – Temporary delay in execution to allow appeal for pardon. Delay in eve.
                                       * Art 74 - Article 74 – There shall be CoM headed by the PM to aid and advise the president. As per 42nd CAA, such advice is binding. As per 44th CAA, the president may require CoM to reconsider such advice and shall act in accordance with CoM's advice after reconsideration.
                                       * Art 75 - CoM = collectively responsible
                                       * Conditions of Office
                                       * No membership in Parlia/State Legislature-  must vacate a seat if holding one before taking oath or the day he enters upon his office as president.
                                       * Emoluments, allowance and Privileges 
                                       * As per the constitution, Determined by Parliament and cannot be diminished to his disadvantage. 
                                       * Residence: Rashtrapati Bhavan (rent-free).
                                       * Entitlements: Salary, allowances, furnished residence, car, medical, travel, secretarial staff.
                                       * Legal Immunity:
                                       * Criminal: Complete immunity.
                                       * Civil: Allowed after 2 months' notice (for personal acts). (Same as Governor)
                                       * Post-Retirement:
                                       * Spouse of the deceased President gets 50% pension + other benefits.
                                       * Term - 5 years from the date of entering office. 
                                       * Vacancy 
                                       * By Expiration: Fixed term ends; outgoing President continues if successor is delayed (to prevent interregnum).
                                       * By Resignation, Removal, Death, or Otherwise:
                                       *  Must be filled within six months. 
                                       * Until then, the VP acts as President but does not assume the office. 
                                       * If the VP is unavailable, CJI discharges the President’s functions with the same powers, immunity, and emoluments under the President (Discharge of Functions) Act, 1969. This scenario has never occurred in India.
                                       * Note- Resignation to VP, CJI in his absence
  
Powers of prez
                                       1. Executive powers (appointment + admin powers)
                                       1. Appointment
                                       1. PM, Ministers, governor
                                       2. CAG, CEC, UPSC, EC, FC ( members + chair )
                                       3. Inter state council
                                       4. Commission for SC/ ST / OBC 
                                       2. Admin
                                       1. UT via administrator
                                       2. 5th Scheduled areas - power wrt its admin. 
                                       1. Can declare any area = Scheduled area 
                                       3. Exec decisions
                                       1. Taken In his name
                                       2. Makes rule for authentication of order in his name
                                       3. Appoint ambassador (on council of minister’s advice), High Commissioner – Represents India in Commonwealth countries 
                                       2. Legislative powers
                                       1. Summon, Prorogue, Dissolve Lok Sabha; call joint sitting (Speaker presides).
                                       2. Address first Parliament session post-election.
                                       3. Send messages on pending bills to both Houses (not by parliamentary secretariat) 
                                       4. Appoint RS / LS members to preside if Speaker / Deputy Speaker & Chairman / Deputy Chairman posts are vacant.
                                       5. Nominate RS members with expertise (Art. 80(2), 4th Schedule).
                                       6. Prior recommendation for bills affecting expenditure, state boundaries, or inter-state trade.
                                       7. Assent/Return Bill (except Money Bill); must approve if repassed.
                                       8. State Bills: Assent/Return (except Money Bill); no obligation on repassage.
                                       9. Promulgate ordinances (art 123)
                                       10. Lay reports before Parliament.
                                       11. Regulate peace & governance in UTs (separate from executive power where he administers it).
                                       3. Ordinance power
                                       1. Origin = GoI act 1935. Art 123 empowers Prez to promulgate ordinances during recess of parliament. They have the same force & effect of the act of Parliament but are in the nature of temporary laws. 
                                       2. Four Limitation
                                       1. Recess- Only when either/ both houses not in session
                                       1. If made when both house is in session, ordinance is void
                                       2. President is Satisfied that circumstances require immediate action
                                       1. In Cooper Case 1970, SC held that his Satisfaction is subjected to judicial review if malafide ( JR restored by 44th CAA)
                                       3. Coextensive but not parallel power with parliament as-
                                       1. Coextensive -
                                       1. issued only on subjects where parliament can make laws. 
                                       2. Subject to the same constitutional limitation as Parliament i.e. can't be ultra vires FRs. 
                                       3. Can be retrospective- come into force from back date
                                       4. Can be Issued in war/ peace / emergency.
                                       2. Duration Limit: temporary and valid for 6 months
                                       3. Law-making Authority: Unlike Parliament, the President cannot make permanent laws.
                                       4. Not for CAA.
                                       4. Must lay before and approved by both houses after reassembly-  within 6 weeks of its session (At most 7.5 months total without parliamentary approval)
                                       5. Lok Sabha rules require a Statement justifying an ordinance-replacing Bill. No SC case has challenged presidential re-promulgation. 


Re-promulgation of Ordinances: Misuse & Key Cases
                                       * Bihar (1967-1981): Over 256 ordinances were re-promulgated, some like land reforms ordinances lasting 15+ years (noted in D.C. Wadhwa v. State of Bihar, 1987).
                                       * The Securities Laws (Amendment) Ordinance, 2014, was re-promulgated thrice, sparking concerns over bypassing legislative approval.
                                       * Other states like Rajasthan, Odisha, and Punjab have also frequently re-promulgated ordinances.
Misuse of Ordinance-Making Power
                                       * Bypassing the Legislature – D.C. Wadhwa case (1987) case noted that ordinances should be for urgent situations, re-promulgation violates legislative supremacy 
                                       * Lack of Debate & Transparency – Example: Farm Laws (2020) were first introduced via ordinances, avoiding parliamentary debate.
                                       * Undermining Separation of Powers – Example: Triple Talaq Ordinance (2018) – Issued multiple times before legislative approval, raising concerns over executive overreach.
3. Conclusion
                                       * SC in Krishna Kumar Singh (2017) held that re-promulgation is a fraud on the Constitution.
                                       * Ordinance-making should be an exception, not a rule, to uphold legislative supremacy and democratic accountability.
	

                                       4. Financial powers
                                       1. Money bills 
                                       1. Cause the budget to be laid
                                       2. Recommend/ approve demand for grant 
                                       2. financial bills
                                       3. Constitute Finance commission - appoints, set terms of reference, 
                                       4. Make Advance out of contingency fund to beat unforeseen expenditure
                                       5. Establishes GST council
                                       5. Judicial powers
                                       1. Appoint CJI, SC/HC judge
                                       2. Seek unbinding advice - SC on question of law Article 143
                                       3. Article 72- Grant of Pardons
                                       6. Diplomatic power
                                       1. International treaties, agreements signed- his name, subjected to parlia approval
                                       2. Represent india in intl forum, fare
                                       3. Send + receive diplomats. 
                                       7. Military powers 
                                       1. Supreme commander (3 armed forces)
                                       2. Appoint Army, navy, air force chief
                                       3. Declare war, conclude peace
                                       4. All power are subjected to parliament approval ( A 53 )
                                       8. Veto powers
                                       1. It legal power to unilaterally stop official action
                                       2. 24th amendment - took away veto power for CAA .So it Mandatory to give assent for CAA
                                       3. (done in parliament chapter) 
 
Article 53 – The executive power of the Union shall be vested in the President and exercised by them directly or through subordinate officers as per the Constitution.
Importance- President is not a rubber stamp
                                       1. Preserver protector and defender of Constitution and law (Art 60) – E.g., 1987, President Zail Singh refused immediate assent to the Indian Post Office (Amendment) Bill, citing concerns over potential misuse and its impact on citizens' rights.
                                       2. Preserves security and integrity of India
                                       1. Emergency Powers (Article 352, 356, 360)– Ex: 1975 Emergency approval by President Fakhruddin Ali Ahmed; Ex 2: President Draupadi Murmu’s approval of President approval in Manipur following internal conflicts. 
                                       2. Commander-in-Chief of the Armed Forces (Article 53): Example: In 1999, during the Kargil War, President K.R. Narayanan oversaw key military decisions.
                                       3. Legislative powers
                                       1. Suspensive Veto on Ordinary Bills (Article 111)– In 2005, President A.P.J. Abdul Kalam returned the Office of Profit Bill for reconsideration, showing that the President is not just a ceremonial figure but has constitutional authority.
                                       2. Ordinance making power (Art 123)-  Muslim Women (Protection of Rights on Marriage) Second Ordinance, 2019 promulgated by Ramnath Kovind which criminalized the practice of instant triple talaq.
                                       4. Situational discretion
                                       1. Government Formation in a Hung Parliament (Article 75): Example: After Indira Gandhi’s assassination, Giani Zail Singh appointed Rajiv Gandhi as PM, bypassing convention.
                                       2. Government Removal (Article 75(2)): Can dismiss the CoM if it loses confidence.
                                       * Dismissal of CoM does not lead to dismissal of LS. New PM can be appointed.
                                       3. Dissolution of Lok Sabha (Article 85(2)(b)): Can dissolve the Lok Sabha if no government secures a majority.
                                       * Dissolution of LS leads to dismissal of CoM itself but they continue office until fresh elections.
	Vice president (article 63-71) 
                                       *  2nd highest office in india
                                       * Article 63: Establishes the office of the Vice President of India.
                                       * Article 64: The Vice President is the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha and cannot hold any other office of profit.
                                       * Election (article 66)
                                       * Originally election=both houses of parl@ joint meeting. 11th CAA removed it
                                       * Electoral college
                                       * Both elected + nominated parliament members elect
                                       * No SL members  (as VP preside over state council rarely, temporarily, thus no necessity- BR Ambedkar)
                                       * Value- Only MPs take part, hence the value of vote of each MP is 1. 
                                       * Election commission, in consultation with CG, appoints the Secretary General of LS and RS, by rotation, as Returning Officer for election of VP.  
                                       * Process - 
                                       * Election by PR using STV with a secret ballot.
                                       * Election disputes inquired & decided by SC (final decision) 
                                       * Election not challengeable on the grounds that electoral college incomplete
                                       * If election declared invalid, acts done by him before declaration are not invalidated
                                       * Facts - 
                                       * Only twice elected- Dr. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan was twice elected as the Vice President of India (1952–1962) that too unopposed, and Dr. Rajendra Prasad was twice elected as the President of India (1950–1962).
                                       * Qualifications
                                       * Indian citizen, 35 years of age
                                       * Qualified for election to RS
                                       * No office of profit (Undefined in consti) under union/ state government,  local / public authority
                                       * Sitting Prez or VP , governor of any state, minister for union or state is NOT deemed to hold any office of profit , hence qualified for being a candidate 
                                       * 20 electors as proposers as well as seconders, 15,000/- as deposit in RBI. 
                                       * Oath (article 69) 
                                       * Bear true faith and allegiance to constitution & faithfully discharge duties.
                                       * Oath administered by the President/ some person appointed by him.
                                       * (Note: for 3rd Schedule like MPs- bear true faith and allegiance to the Constitution of India and uphold the sovereignty and integrity of the country)
                                       * Conditions of office- 
                                       * Should not be a member of Either Parliament House or state Legislature. If any such  elected- deemed to have vacated- On entry date of office.
                                       * No other office of profit.
                                       * Term & Tenure (article 67)
                                       * 5 years from date on entering office
                                       * VP have security of tenure and has fixed tenure
                                       * VP can hold office beyond 5 years to ensure continuity, unless successor assumes office
                                       * No formal impeachment process needed-
                                       * Only removed by resolution passed by effective majority of all RS members present & voting, agreed to by LS (simple majority) 
                                       * 14 day’s advance notice for resolution 
                                       * Eligible for re-election any number of time
                                       * Vacancy in office- 
                                       * Tenure expiry, resignation, removal, death, or disqualification.
                                       * In case of Vacancy due to term Expiry: Election must be held before term ends (Article 68)
                                       * Resignation:
                                       * President resigns to VP; in VP’s absence, to CJI.
                                       * VP resigns to President.
                                       * Powers & fn
                                       * Ex-officio chairman (RS) Article 64 - similar power & fn as LS speaker (resemble american VP- who acts as chairman of upper senate)
                                       * Act as President if vacancy in office- Max 6 month
                                       * Dont perform duty of RS chairman while acting as Prez or discharging function (which are instead performed by Deputy Chairman of RS)
                                       * Emoluments as per 2nd schedule of consti provides that salaries for all the following offices cannot be varied to their disadvantage.
                                       * For President, VP, Governors, Presiding Officers, and CAG: Salaries are determined by Parliament through specific laws now. (So consti doesnt fix the salary of the VP, Parliament does so.)
                                       * For Supreme Court and High Court Judges: The Constitution still provides for their salaries, but Parliament can modify them by law.
India
	American
	Neither  Succeed, nor assume the office of President upon vacancy, merely serves as “acting president” for max 6 months. 
	Succeed upon vacancy & remain so for unexpired term of predecessor
	

The Vice President of India, the second-highest constitutional office, is established under Article 63. Primarily a ceremonial role, the VP serves as Rajya Sabha Chairman and acts as President in contingencies, ensuring political continuity.
Importance of Vice President’s Position in India
Executive role- 
                                       * Guardian of political Continuity– . Ex: In 1969, when President Zakir Husain passed away, Vice President V.V. Giri served as Acting President. 
                                       * Bridge Between Legislature & Executive. Example: In 1979, VP B.D. Jatti’s crucial mediation between Janata govt & RS, ensuring stability amid political turmoil
Rajya Sabha’s Umpire – 
                                       * Presiding Over Rajya Sabha– (Article 89). Example: In 2023, VP Jagdeep Dhankhar firmly  maintained legislative discipline over the Delhi Services Bill, ensuring a structured debate despite opposition protests.
                                       * Referring Bills to Committees- Ex: Rules committee, business advisory com.
                                       * Casting Vote in Case of Tie – Votes only in case of a deadlock (Article 100).
                                       * Maintaining Discipline– Example:In 2024, VP Jagdeep Dhankhar reprimanded Jaya Bachchan for her aggressive remarks in Rajya Sabha (Rule 255 of Rajya Sabha).
                                       * Disqualification of Members– (10th Schedule)
Note- Write ans for Once a speaker, always a speaker.
	Governor 
                                       * Article 153-167, PART 6 deal - state executive - governor, CM, CoM, Adv gen
                                       * A 154 – Executive power of the state vests in the Governor.
                                       * 7th CAA 1956 - held validity of appointment of same person as governor @ 2 or more state
                                       * Article 163(1)– It states that the Governor is to act on the aid and advice of the CoMs except in cases where the Constitution requires the Governor to act in his discretion.
                                       * 42nd CAA – Made CoM’s advice binding on the President; but it didn't explicitly mention for governor b/c it was already constitutionally binding.
                                       * SC in Samsher singh and Nabam rebia cases also reiterated that same principle applies to Governors
                                       * A 164 – CoM is collectively responsible to the State Legislative Assembly, ensuring a parliamentary system.
                                       * A 174 – Governor summons the legislature at a time & place deemed fit.
                                       * A 175 – Governor can send messages to the legislature on pending bills.
                                       * A 176 – Governor delivers a special address twice: after general elections & at the first session of the year.
                                       * A 200 – Governor’s assent is needed for a bill to become law; may also return or reserve it for the President.

                                          * Appointment of Governor
                                          * Model Adopted: Indirect appointment (Canadian model) over direct election (American model).
                                          * Appointed by the President under his warrant (Article 155).
                                          * SC (1973) hargovind pant case: Constitutional office, not under CG control.
                                          * Holds office during the President's pleasure (Article 156).
                                          * Reasons Against Direct Election (Constituent Assembly Debate)
                                          * Incompatible with the parliamentary system in states.
                                          * May cause conflict with CM.
                                          * Unnecessary cost for a nominal post.
                                          * Bridge between centre and state- Partisan affiliation risks neutrality.
                                          * Oath of office (Article 159)
                                          * Administered by Chief Justice of HC 
                                          * - Same as Prez- PPD
                                          * Only 2 Qualifications
                                          * Indian citizen, 
                                          * 35+ years old (Article 157).
                                          * Conventions regarding appointment (Often Violated)
                                          * Should be an outsider to avoid local political influence.
                                          * President to consult the CM for smooth functioning. (not necessary)
                                          * Conditions of Governor’s Office-
                                          * Holds office at President’s pleasure (Article 156).
                                          * Not a member of Parliament or State Legislature; if appointed, seat vacated before taking oath (Article 158).
                                          * No office of profit (Article 158).
                                          * Emoluments 
                                          * Official residence (Raj Bhavan) without rent.
                                          * Emoluments, allowances, and privileges determined by Parliament; if serving in two states, shared by both states (Article 158)
                                          * Salary & allowances cannot be diminished during tenure (Article 158).
                                          * Legal immunity for official acts (Article 361)
                                          * No criminal proceedings for personal acts during tenure.
                                          * Civil proceedings only after a two-month notice.
                                          * Removal
                                          * Can be removed by the President at any time, no grounds of removal (Article 156).
                                          * Sarkaria Commission: upheld ‘at pleasure’ doctrine but Governors should not be removed before tenure completion except in rare and compelling cases.
                                          * Term of Governor’s Office
                                          * Tenure: 5 years
                                          * No security of tenure; can resign anytime.
                                          * No constitutional grounds for removal; which is at President's discretion.
                                          * The President may transfer the Governor to another state (Article 156).
                                          * Continues beyond 5 years until successor takes charge to prevent interregnum.
                                          * If the office falls vacant, the Chief Justice of the state High Court may be appointed temporarily (Article 160).
Powers
                                          1. Exec power
                                          * Administrative Authority- same as prez
                                          * Appointments & Removals
                                          * Appoints CM & CoM (hold office at pleasure).
                                          * Tribal Welfare Minister mandatory in Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, MP, Odisha.
                                          * Appoints Advocate General of state (holds office at pleasure).
                                          * Appoints State Election Commissioner..
                                          * Appoints Chairman & Members of State PSC (removable only by President).
                                          * Special Powers
                                          * Recommends constitutional emergency (President’s Rule), assuming extensive executive power.
                                          * Chancellor of state universities; appoints Vice Chancellors.
                                          2. Legislative power-  same as prez
                                          * Reports to Legislature- State Finance Commission, PSC, and CAG reports before Legislature.
                                          * Can't make ordinance without president’s approval in 3 cases
                                          * If the ordinance requires Presidential assent as a bill under art 200
                                          * If it affects the jurisdiction of the High Court.
                                          * If it contradicts a central law in the Concurrent List.
                                          3. Fin powers- Same as prez
                                          4. Judicial powers
                                          * Governor lacks powers under Article 161 in cases of:
                                          * Court-martial sentences, Death sentences (Only President can grant pardon)
                                          * Judicial Appointments
                                          * Consulted by President for HC judge appointments.
                                          * Appoints, posts, and promotes District Judges in consultation with HC.
                                          * Appoints judicial officers (except District Judges) in consultation with HC & State PSC.
                                          * Presidential Intervention on Errant Governors- President K.R Narayanan’s Actions: 2001: Recommended recalling TN Governor Fathima Beevi after inaction during Karunanidhi’s arrest
                                          1. SR Bommai case (1994) mandated the floor test as the sole way to prove majority, limiting the Governor’s power to dismiss state governments.)

	as per direction by the president. Consultations of CoM Required but acts finally on his own discretion

	Prime Minister 
Article 75- governs the appointment, tenure, and responsibilities of the CoM, including the Prime Minister:
                                          1. Art 75 (1)- Appointment: The President appoints the PM, and other ministers on the PM’s advice. Total ministers ≤ 15% LS strength (91st Amendment).
                                          1. Parliamentary conventions-
                                          1. No election provision in the Constitution.The President appoints the Lok Sabha majority leader as PM.
                                          2. If no party has a clear majority in LS, the President may exercise his personal discretion (usually largest party/coalition & ask him to seek a Vote of Confidence within a reasonable time, usually set by the President. (Ex: Charan singh in 1979 for 1st time)
                                          1. S.R. Bommai case (1994): A floor test, not the President/Governor, determines the majority.
                                          3. When a PM dies without an obvious successor, Prez can exercise his personal discretion. Ex- In 1984, Prez Zail singh appointed Rajiv Gandhi after assassination of Indira gandhi  ignoring Precedent of appointing caretaker PM
                                          4. 1980 HC ruling: No need to prove majority before appointment; proof comes later.
                                          5. 1997 SC Ruling- Can be from either House, unlike Britain ( Lower House). Rajya Sabha MPs as PM
                                          1. Indira Gandhi (after Shastri's death).
                                          2. HD Deve Gowda, IK Gujral, Dr. Manmohan Singh.
                                          2. Art 75 (2) Tenure: Ministers hold office during the President’s pleasure, but must have Lok Sabha’s confidence. (No fixed term mentioned)
                                          3. Art 75 (3) Collective Responsibility: 
                                          1. Collective responsibility means as a team, they swim & sink together. The CoM is collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha (can be removed via no-confidence motion). 
                                          1. PM resign as per convention, Not consti requirement
                                          2. Individual responsibility- Also means that  if any minister disagrees with cabinet ministers, and he’s not prepared to defend it, he must resign. Alternatively, The president may remove any minister on PM’s advice in case of difference in opinions.
                                          3. No legal responsibility unlike British
                                          4. Art 75 (4) Oath & Salary: Ministers take an oath (Third Schedule) administered by the President, and Parliament decides their salaries.
                                          1. True faith & allegiance to the Constitution.
                                          2. Uphold sovereignty & integrity.
                                          3. Discharge duties faithfully & conscientiously.
                                          4. Do right to all manner of people in accordance with consti and law without fear, favor, affection, or ill will.
                                          5. Maintain secrecy; no unauthorized disclosure.
                                          5. Article 75(5)- A person who is not an MP can be appointed as a Minister (or PM). They must become an MP within 6 months, or they cease to be a Minister.
                                          * Conditions of Service (Parliamentary convention)
                                          * Term: 5 years, reappointment allowed.
                                          * If LS dissolves, the PM continues at the President's request.
                                          * Defeat in LS: Must resign with the Cabinet.
                                          * Defeat in RS: No mandatory resignation.
                                          * Holds office at President’s pleasure but that doesnt mean prez can remove anytime, ie PM can’t be dismissed if holding a majority. 
                                          * Powers & Functions of PM
                                          * Legislative:
                                          * Leader of House and MP.
                                          * Art 74- Aid and advice- Consulted by the President for convening/ proroguing Parliament.
                                          * Article 85(2) - Recommends LS dissolution.
                                          * Council of Ministers:
                                          * Cabinet members appointed on his recommendation.
                                          * Allocates & reshuffles portfolios.
                                          * Can ask ministers to resign or advise dismissal.
                                          * Presides over meetings, influencing decisions.
                                          * Directs, coordinates, and controls ministerial activities.
                                          * Resignation/ death dissolves the CoM.
                                          * Articles for CoM
                                          * Relation with President:
                                          * Article 74 – There shall be CoM headed by the PM to aid and advise the president. As per 42nd CAA, such advice is binding. As per 44th CAA, president may require CoM to reconsider such advice and shall act in accordance to CoM’s advice after reconsideration.
                                          * 1971- SC = after LS dissolution , CoM doesn't cease automatically  
                                          * Article 74(2) - advice tended to the president is NOT subject to JR
                                          * Article 75
                                          * Art 77
                                          * All executive action to be taken in name of President
                                          * Article 78 – PM’s duties:
                                          * Informing the President about decisions of the CoM.
                                          * Providing information on Union affairs when asked.
                                          * Submitting matters for reconsideration by the Council if required by the President.
                                          * Relation with Parliament:
                                          * Advises on summoning/proroguing Parliament.
                                          * Recommends LS dissolution.
                                          * Announces govt. policies in the House.
                                          * Other Powers:
                                          * Chairman of 
                                          * NITI Aayog
                                          * Interstate council (Art 263) 
                                          * National integration council
                                          * National Water resources council 
                                          * NGRBA 
                                          * National commission for population control 
                                          * Nuclear command authority
                                          * NDMA
                                          * Indian Board for wildlife -IBW (animal welfare board of india is different & is under prevention of crulety act, min of fisheries)
                                          * Dept under PMO 
                                          * Space
                                          * Atomic energy
                                          * R&AW
                                          * Cabinet secretariat headed by cabinet secretary 
                                          * CSIR
                                          * Council of Climate research 
                                          * Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser (PSA)- Prime Minister S&T, Innovation Advisory Council (PM-STIAC)
                                          * Shapes foreign policy.
                                          * Chief  spokesperson of the union govt. 
                                          * Crisis manager during emergencies. 
Prime Minister- De Facto head of executive
Lord Morley described the Prime Minister (PM) as “primus inter pares” (first among equals) and the keystone of the cabinet arch, highlighting the PM’s centrality in India’s parliamentary democracy. The PM leads the CoM, acts as a bridge between the President and Parliament, and directs national policy. 
Executive Powers
                                          * Article 74- Leads the CoM, advises the President . Post-42nd & 44th Amendments, the President must act on reconsidered advice.
                                          * Art 75- Allocates portfolios, reshuffles, and dismisses ministers.
                                          * Chairs NITI Aayog, NDMA, Nuclear Command Authority, and Interstate Council.
Legislative Powers
                                          * Article 85- Advises on summoning/dissolution of Lok Sabha.
                                          * Chief spokesperson of the government, announces policies.
Challenges and Reforms
1. Coalition Chaos – Short-Lived Governments
🔹 Issue: Frequent floor tests disrupt governance (S.R. Bommai case, 1994). e.g., Gujral, Deve Gowda in the 1990s
✅ Solution: Strengthen coalition agreements and enforce clear majority-proof rules.
________________
2. Presidential Discretion – Who Becomes PM in a Crisis?
🔹 Issue: No clear constitutional guidelines on PM appointment during sudden vacancies or a hung Parliament(e.g., Rajiv Gandhi’s appointment in 1984).
✅ Solution: Define presidential discretion and enforce a time-bound floor test (Article 75(1), S.R. Bommai case, 1994).
________________
3. Backdoor PMs – A Question of Legitimacy
🔹 Issue: 1997 SC ruling allows RS members as PM, raising concerns about democratic representation (e.g., Manmohan Singh, 2004).
✅ Solution: Amend Article 75(5) to require PMs from Lok Sabha or mandate a floor test within 30 days.
________________
4. PM office Overreach  – Sidelines ministers
🔹 Ex: e.g., Indira Gandhi’s Emergency, 1975-77.
✅ Solution: Decentralize decision-making and strengthen ministerial independence (Article 75(3)).
Conclusion- The Prime Minister, as head of govt, must wield executive authority while maintaining Parliamentary accountability. The evolving political challenges call for reforms to ensure stability and uphold democratic integrity.
	Council of Ministers 
                                          * Art- 74
                                          * Art 75
                                          * Art 77
                                          * Art 78
                                          * Art 88- every minister shall have right to speak and take part in proceedings of either house & joint sitting & any committee of which she is named a member, but she’s not entitled to vote
                                          * Oath- same as PM
                                          * Composition of council of ministers (as per conventions)- The diff lies in respective ranks, emoluments, and political importance. 
                                          * Origin- Britain
                                          * Cabinet Ministers – Head key ministries, shape policy, oversee CG. May also include Deputy PM. 
                                          * State Ministers – Independent charge of ministry/ dept or attached to Cabinet Minister; (same powers as Cabinet Minister) don’t attend cabinet meetings unless invited.
                                          * Deputy Ministers – No independent charge of ministry/dept & attached to cabinet ministers; assist in administration, politics, and parliament
                                          * Parliamentary Secretaries – Part of ministry, no control, assist senior ministers in parliamentary duties. ( Not been appointed since 1967




Kitchen Cabinet
A kitchen cabinet is an informal group of 2-4 trusted advisors, including outsiders like friends and family, who assist the head of government in decision-making. It originated in the U.S.
1. Swift Decision-Making vs. Cabinet Marginalization
✅ Ex: Indira Gandhi relied on P.N. Haksar for strategic decisions, ensuring efficiency.
❌ Ex: The National Advisory Council (NAC) under Sonia Gandhi influenced policy, overshadowing Manmohan Singh’s cabinet.
🛠 Sol: The Punchhi Commission recommended clearly defining advisory roles to prevent excessive influence while ensuring quick decision-making.
2. Crisis Response vs. Accountability Concerns
✅ Ex: Ajit Doval’s role in the 2019 Balakot airstrikes enabled rapid crisis response.
❌ Ex: Sanjay Gandhi, without formal authority, exerted undue influence during the Emergency (1975-77).
🛠 Sol The NCRWC suggested parliamentary committees should oversee informal advisors to maintain accountability.
3. Policy Secrecy vs. Transparency Needs
✅ Example: The 2016 demonetization plan was executed secretly by a select group.
🛠 Sol: 2nd ARC recommended a legal framework for confidential policymaking with post-facto parliamentary review to ensure both secrecy and transparency.

 Cabinet Committees
                                          * Extra-constitutional bodies, formed by rules of business.
                                          * Types:
                                          * Standing Committees (Permanent).
                                          * Ad Hoc Committees (Temporary).
                                          * Formed by PM, varies by need.
                                          * Members: 3-8 (Mostly cabinet ministers, non-cabinet are also allowed).
                                          * Functions: Can make decisions.
                                          * Important Cabinet Committees
                                          * Headed by PM
                                          * Political Affairs
                                          * Economic Affairs
                                          * Appointments
                                          * Security
                                          *  Investment and Growth
                                          * Employment & Skill Development:
                                          * Head by MHA
                                          * Accomodation.
                                          * Headed by Defence Minister
                                          * Parliamentary Affairs
                                          * Group of Ministers (GoM)
                                          * Appointed under Govt of India (Transaction of Business) Rules, 1961
                                          * Ad-hoc panels- Some GoM take decisions on behalf of the cabinet & others make recommendations formed by the PM or Cabinet.
                                          * Single-window clearance for cross-ministry policymaking & governance.
                                          * 2nd ARC flagged a large number of GoMs resulting in many GoMs not being able to meet regularly causing significant delays. 
                                          * Recommended selective but effective use of GOms with clear mandate and prescribed timelines. 
Council of state 
                                          * Article 163: CoM & Governor- Governor acts on ministerial aid & advice, except in discretionary matters, where his decision is final.
                                          * Courts cannot question the validity of ministerial advice given to the Governor.
                                          * Article 164: Appointment of Minister. 
                                          * CM appointed by Governor; other ministers appointed on CM's advice.
                                          * Article 166: Conduct of State Govt Business
                                          * Same as PM & President at Union level.
                                          * Article 177: Minister’s Rights in Legislature
                                          * Ministers can speak & participate in Assembly, Council, and Committees.
                                          * SC Rulings:
                                          * 1974: Governor must act on aid & advice, except in discretionary powers. No personal discretion.


Chief Minister
                                          * A 164 - governor appoints CM. Uses situational discretion when no clear majority:
                                          * No constitutional laws- 
                                          * Governor appoints CM; Can appoint largest party leader or coalition head & ask to prove majority usually within a month.
                                          * No pre-appointment majority proof required; must prove within a set period as decided by governor 
                                          * On CM’s death: Party elects a new leader; Governor has no choice of discretion.
                                          * Non-legislator CM: Can serve 6 months, must become MLA/MLC or ceases to hold office.
                                          * Other powers
                                          * Chairman -  state planning board
                                          * Vice chairman - zonal council
                                          * Member - inter state council/ NDC headed by PM
                                          * Meet ppl as leader of state
                                          * Total number of ministers, including CM < 15% total strength of assembly. Not less than 12 ( 91st CAA 2003 )
