	FEDERALISM 
                                          * Federalism, as per Sarkaria commission, is more of a functional arrangement for co-operative action than a static institutional concept of power sharing between multiple levels of government. In the Bommai Case (1994), SC had included federalism in the basic structure doctrine.
                                          * Based on Canadian Model
                                          * Article 1: India is a "Union of States," implying an indestructible federation with no right for states to secede.
                                          * 7th Schedule: Defines the division of powers through the Union, State, and Concurrent Lists.
                                          * Centripetal tendencies: Formed through disintegration. India has strong centralizing forces ensuring national unity.
                                          * Different from USA Model
                                          * Holding together federation: India follows this model, unlike the USA’s "coming together" federation.
                                          * Not a result of state agreement: Unlike the USA


Quasi Federalism of India- A critical Evaluation
                                          * KC Wheare described India as quasi federal where it has unitary features with subsidiary federal features. 
                                          * Ivor Jennings-described India as a federation with strong centralising tendencies. 
                                          * BR Ambedkar stressed on constitutional supremacy & states in no way are dependent on Centre for their legislative & exec authority. 
	Federal features of Indian Consti
	Unitary Features of Indian consti/ Trends in centre- state relations
	                                          * Dual polity- most imp- national & state govt
                                          * Division of powers- 7th schedule- Clearly specified jurisdiction of each tier. The Union list has matters of national imp like defence, foreign affairs, and communication. State list like health, public order. Concurrent list.
                                          * Written, Rigid &  Supremacy of constitution- Art 368- state ratification
                                          * Independent judiciary- upholds consti
                                          * Bicameral Legislature- Rajya sabha maintains federal equilibrium by rep the interest of states.
                                          * Cooperative federalism- Granville Austin
                                          * Interstate council.
                                          * Non constitutional devices like NITI Aayog, National Integration council, Zonal Councils, UGC
                                          * Confrontational Federalism 
                                          * Territorial disputes ex Belgaum
                                          * Water sharing issues ex -SYL issue
                                          * Regional parties ex- DMK 
                                          * Regionalism ex- linguistic states formation like Andhra 
                                          * Resistance to central laws by states ex- 3 farm laws crisis
	                                          * Indestructible union of destructible States- states have no right to territorial integrity- Parliament can alter/ create new by simple majority under art 3.
                                          * Strong centre- 
                                          * 98 subjects in the union list
                                          * 59 subjects on the state list. Encroachment of parliament on state list ex: 3 farm laws. 
                                          * Overriding powers on concurrent lists.
                                          * Residuary powers with parliament.
                                          * Office Governor
                                          * Agent of centre- Holds office during pleasure of prez. Ex:  Enquiry committees against CMs by governor/ President.
                                          * Consti and situational discretion- Ex: 200
                                          * The president has absolute Veto over state bills reserved by the governor.
                                          * Emergency provisions- 
                                          * National emergency ( 352)
                                          * prez rule ( 356 )
                                          * Deployment of central forces - Ex AFSPA in Manipur and Nagaland
                                          * Dominance of centre in financial sphere- K Santhanam. 
                                          * Major taxation powers like IT, GST in hands of CG.
                                          * Corporation Tax is part of divisible pool
                                          * Discrimination in financial allocation between states. Ex: UP vs Maharashtra
                                          * Grant in aid at discretion of Parliament on advice of FC. (GranPa Finances)
                                          * State borrowing within india - centre regulate
                                          * Single & Flexible constitution.
                                          * Integrated Judiciary, CAG, ECI, AIS (State cant remove, disciplinary action - central civil servants- but they are reqd b/c they provide uniformity and bridge between centre and state)
                                          * No equality of state rep- based on population. 
	Conclusion- 
Sarkaria commission on centre state relations (1983-88) observed the rule of union supremacy is a technique to safeguard national unity and integrity which is being threatened by fissiparous tendencies in the body politic. 
	  

Modernisation: The Punchhi Commission went further by addressing economic liberalisation (review of cess and surcharge system), internal security (specialised structure for communal violence and migration issues), and environmental concerns (National env protection authority), which were not major focus areas in the 1980s
Commissions on Centre- state relations

                                          1. 2nd ARC 1966 ( under morarji desai)- No action taken
                                          1. Inter-state council under Article 263- establish it.
                                          2. Governor- persons having long-experiences in public life and non-partisan attitudes 
                                          3. 7th schedule- Delegate max subjects to states.
                                          4. Fiscal decentralisation to states to reduce central dependence centres.

                                             2. Rajmannar commission (1969- TN- No action taken)
                                             1. Inter state council
                                             2. Emergency-Delete article dealing with President’s rule articles  A. 356, 357, 365
                                             3. Abolish AIS ( bridge between centre & states)
                                             4. Replace PC by statutory body

                                                3. Anandpur sahib resolution (1973, Akali Dal- No action taken)
                                                1. Residuary powers to state
                                                2. Centre’s power to be restricted to defence, foreign, communication and currency.

                                                   4. West bengal memorandum - 1977 - IGNORED!!

                                                   5. Sarkaria commission 1983-1988
                                                      1. RS Sarkaria, Retired judge of SC- did not favour structural changes but focussed on functional & operational changes.- 180/ 247 recommendations accepted.
                                                      2. Favoured Centralising tendencies for unity and integrity.
                                                      3. Art 263- Setting up a permanent inter-state council.
                                                      4. Article 356 should be used sparingly.
                                                      5. Governor powers
                                                      1. Presidential veto on state bills- Reasons should be communicated to the state.
                                                      2. Governor’s appointment- Consultation of CM by Prez should be constitutionalised.
                                                      3. CM- No commission of enquiry against state minister unless parliament demands 
                                                      6. Financial Matters
                                                      1. Corporation tax should be shared with states and no Surcharge on Income Tax.
                                                      7. 7th schedule- Residuary powers of taxation should continue with parliament while others should be placed under concurrent list. And the centre should consult the state before making law on concurrent list.
                                                      8. Implement 3 language formula; Commissioner for linguistic minorities should be activated.
                                                      9. No change in the role of RS & centre’s power to reorganise the states. 
                                                      10. AIS should be strengthened.
                                                      11. The Centre should have powers to deploy its armed forces, even without the consent of states. However, it is desirable that the states should be consulted (AFSPA)

                                                         1. Punchhi commission 
                                                         1. 2007-10 , under Madan Mohan Punchhi, ex-CJI, 310 recommendations 
                                                         2. Favoured cooperative federalism
                                                         3. President’s Rule (Article 356)
                                                         1. Use only as a last resort and Prior warning to the state before imposition.
                                                         2. Parliamentary consultation before dissolving the state legislature and Judicial review to prevent misuse.
                                                         4.  Governor
                                                         1. Appointment- Eminent and Non-political background, CM consultation before appointment.
                                                         2. Removal- Fixed 5-year tenure, Similar procedure for removal as president.
                                                         3. Limited discretionary powers like Mandatory floor test of CM in case of political instability to avoid centre’s pol interference. 
                                                         4. President’s Veto on State Bills- No indefinite withholding, 6 month Time-bound decision on reserved bills. Reasons must be communicated.
                                                         5. 7th Schedule (Division of Powers)
                                                         1. Review Union, State, and Concurrent Lists as per evolving needs.
                                                         2. State list- More autonomy to states in subjects like education & health.
                                                         3. Concurrent list- Consultation with states by parliament before making law; continuous auditing role of Interstate council. 
                                                         6. Finance Commission (FC) & Fiscal Federalism
                                                         1. Terms of reference for FC should be even-handed between centre & states
                                                         2. Convert FC division to full-fledge dept 
                                                         3. Higher fund devolution to states for balanced development.
                                                         4. FC should coordinate with the GST Council.
	

                                                         * Why strong center
National unity in diversity-  Ex- Partition holocaust necessitated a strong centre to stem the tide of anti-national forces. 
                                                         * Economic development= handled by a strong centre + state coopn to move resources. 
Centre state Relations- Part 11
                                                         * Legislative Relations ( article 245 )- 4 aspects
                                                         * Territorial Extent of Legislation (Art 245):
                                                         * Parliament: Entire/part of India + extraterritorial laws  applicable on Indian citizens & their properties in any part of the world. 
                                                         * State: Only within its territory. 
                                                         * Parliament: Residuary powers (Art 248).
                                                         * Exceptions:
                                                         * Governor : Can exempt 5th Scheduled Areas + Assam  from Parliamentary laws.
                                                         * President (Art 240): Regulates A&N, D&D, D&NH, Lakshadweep, Ladakh (UTs w/o legis)
                                                         * President : Same power for tribal areas (6th Schedule) in Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram (not Assam).
                                                         * Distribution of legislative powers (Art 256) 
                                                         * 3 lists’ Origin - GoI act 1935
                                                         * Union List – Parliament has exclusive executive power (originally 97 subs, now 98- Audit, banking and insurance, communication, inter-state trade 
                                                         * State list- normally states have power. (originally 66 subjects, currently 59) - public order, police & prison, public health & sanitation, agriculture & fisheries, market, gambling
                                                         * Concurrent List – both parliament & state, but parliament laws prevail if conflict ( originally 47, now 52)- CrPC, CPC, marriage & divorce, population control & family planning, socio-eco planning, electricity, newspaper/books/printing press, drugs,  labour, GST(101 CAA)
                                                         * 42nd CAA - 5 subjects transferred concurrently from state- Education, justice, forests, protection of wild animal & birds, Weights & measures.  FeW JEW
                                                         * Parliamentary legislation in state field (5 extraordinary circumstance) - 
                                                         * Article 249 - RS passes a resolution by special majority (2/3rd members P&V, 1 year - doesn't restrict SL power to make law on same matter
                                                         * Article 250 -national emergency ( restrict SL power to make law on same matter) , (law ceases 6 month after emergency) 
                                                         * Article 252- when 2 or more state makes request, applies only to those states. Such laws repealed/ amended only by Parliament, not SLA-  restrict SL power to make law on same matter
                                                         * Article 253 - implement international agreements 
                                                         * During President's Rule- parliament can make law ( operative even after president’s rule)
                                                         * Centre’s control over state legislations
                                                         * article 200- reservation of bill by governor for president’s assent (Absolute veto)
                                                         * Bills on certain matters in state list to be intro only with previous consent of president- Ex: Bills imposing restriction on freedom of trade and commerce
                                                         * Financial emergency- Centre can direct states to reserve money bills for prez consideration. 
                                                         * Administrative/ Executive Relations (Art 256-263)
                                                         * Union list - union executive; State list - state executive; Concurrent list - state executive 
                                                         * Obligation of state & centre
                                                         * 2 restriction on state exec power
                                                         * Compliance with parliamentary law
                                                         * Not impede centre's power in state
                                                         * If fail- Art 356 ( state failure to comply with central order leading constitutional M/C breakdown )
                                                         * Centre's direction to state
                                                         * Construction/ Maintenance of means of communication 
                                                         * Article 350A (States to provide primary education in the mother tongue for linguistic minorities).
                                                         * Centre can direct States on railway protection
                                                         * Execution for welfare of Scheduled Tribes 
                                                         * failure to adhere to these directions can lead to breakdown of constitutional machinery article 365
                                                         * Mutual delegation of function
                                                         * With the state's consent, the president may delegate the centre's executive functions to the state. 
                                                         * With the centre's consent, the governor may delegate state’s executive functions to the centre.
                                                         * Without the state's consent, the parliament can delegate the centre's executive function to the state.
                                                         * State legislature can not do this.
                                                         * Cooperation bw Centre & state
                                                         * Art 262- Parliament can adjudicate wrt water dispute
                                                         * Art 263- Interstate council
                                                         * Full faith & credit clause = public act, record, judicial proceeding ( both centre + state )
                                                         * Scope & Execution: Article 261 requires mutual recognition of public acts, records, and judicial proceedings across all states and union territories. Specifically, final judgments from civil courts in one state are enforceable anywhere in India.
                                                         * Limitations: This clause primarily pertains to civil matters; it does not mandate the enforcement of penal (criminal) laws from other states
                                                         * AIS
                                                         * A 312 authorised parliament to create a new AIS on the basis of the RS resolution passed by special majority type 1 (same as art 249- 2/3rd P&V). 
                                                         * Immediate control with state, ultimate control with centre. 
                                                         * PSC
                                                         * SPSC: Chairman & members appointed by Governor, removed by President.
                                                         * Joint SPSC: Formed for ≥2 states, appointed by the President. Report to the governor. Formed by act of parlia
                                                         * UPSC: Assists state on Governor’s request with President’s approval.
                                                         * UPSC: Manages joint recruitment on request of 2+ states.
                                                         * Integrated judicial system
                                                         * HC judge = Prez
                                                         * Parliament = estb common HC (7th CAA)
                                                         * Emergency (A.352, 356, 360)
                                                         * Other provisions -
                                                         * A 355 = 2 duty on centre
                                                         * Protect state from external/ internal aggression
                                                         * Ensure the govt of every state works as per the constitution. 
                                                         * Prez appoint gov
                                                         * Financial relation (Art 268- 293)
                                                         * Allocation of taxing power- 
                                                         * No tax entry in concurrent list. 
                                                         * Residuary power with Parliament. Ex- gift tax 
                                                         * AT constitution, IT levied & collected by centre & distributed b/w centre & state
                                                         * Constitution’s restrictions on state’s taxing power
                                                         * Professions, trades, callings, and employment tax – Cannot exceed ₹2,500 per annum.
                                                         * Tax on sale of goods and services – Prohibited if sale occurs outside the state or in case of import/export transactions.
                                                         * Tax on electricity consumption/sale – Not allowed if consumed by Centre or Railways or sold to them.
                                                         * Tax on water/electricity – Allowed only on storage, generation, distribution, or sale by a Parliament established authority for regulating/ developing any inter-state river/ river valley. Such a law must be reserved for the president's consideration.
                                                         * Distribution of tax/non tax revenue-
                                                         * Constitutional Provisions & Amendments
                                                         * 101st Amendment (2016): Introduced GST , replacing multiple indirect taxes. Both Parliament & State Legislatures have taxing power on GST.
                                                         * Centre-Levied, State- Collected & Appropriated (Art. 268)
                                                         * Stamp duties on select transactions (bills, cheques, insurance, etc.).
                                                         * Directly goes to states, not in the Consolidated Fund.
                                                         * Centre-Levied & Collected, Assigned to States (Art. 269)
                                                         * Inter-state trade taxes (sale, consignment of goods).
                                                         * Levy & collection of GST in inter-state trade of commerce Article 269A- divided between centre & state as per Parliament on GST council recom. 
                                                         * Centre-Levied & Collected, Distributed to States (Art. 270)
                                                         * All central taxes & duties like IT (except surcharge & cess).
                                                         * The President decides distribution on advice of the Finance Commission.
                                                         * Surcharge for Centre (Art. 271)
                                                         * By and for Centre only.
                                                         * Not applicable to GST.
                                                         * State-Levied & Collected
                                                         * 18 types
                                                         * Excise on alcohol and electricity.
                                                         * All types of property tax like land revenue, building, estate duties and mineral rights
                                                         * stamp duty
                                                         * Road, waterway tax & vehicle tax
                                                         * Includes Alcohol and narcotic drugs but Don't include medicines & toiletries & court fees
                                                         * Distribution of non tax revenue - 
                                                         * Centre - post & telegraph, broadcast, banking, coinage & currency , railways, escheat & lapse
                                                         * State - irrigation, forest, fisheries, escheat & lapse
                                                         * Grant-in aid to states
                                                         * By parliament on recommendations of FC
                                                         * Statutory grants ( A 275 )
                                                         * To states in need and not every state.
                                                         * Charged upon CFI every year
                                                         * Consti also provides for specific grant to promote Welfare of ST 
                                                         * Discretionary grants - 282
                                                         * Both C,S empowered to make grants for any pub interest even if outside legislative competence. 
                                                         * Fulfill plan target.
                                                         * Protection of state interest- Following bills can be introduced in parliament only on Prez’s recommendation- 
                                                         * Bill imposing/ varying  any tax in which states are interested
                                                         * Bill which varies meaning of agricultural income
                                                         * Affects principles on which money is distributed to states
                                                         * Bill imposing surcharge on any tax
                                                         * Borrowing by Centre & States
                                                         * CG can borrow domestically/internationally against the Consolidated Fund of India, within limits fixed by Parliament. 
                                                         * States can borrow domestically against the Consolidated Fund of the State, within legislative limits, but not abroad.
                                                         * CG can provide loans/guarantees; States need Centre’s consent for borrowing if state is under debt of centre. 
                                                         * Inter-govt tax immunity
                                                         * Exemption of Central property from state/local taxation 
                                                         * Everything that has money values of the central govt is exempt from state tax. Parliament can remove this ban though. 
                                                         * Commercial companies created by the centre are not immune from state/ local taxation. 
                                                         * Exemption of state property/ income from Central taxation
                                                         * State property and income is exempted from central tax
                                                         * Property & income of local authorities and commercial operation, customs on import/ export of state and excise on mfg products are not are NOT exempted.
                                                         * Emergencies 
                                                         * National emergency - President can cancel/ reduce finance transfer 
                                                         * Financial emergency- reduce salary of all persons serving in state, reserve all money bills,  financial bills for President’s consideration.
                                                         * Art 292 and 293- Vertical fiscal imbalance - Vertical Fiscal Imbalance (VFI)refers to the mismatch between revenue-raising powers and expenditure responsibilities of different levels of government
                                                         * Ex: Centre imposed net borrowing limit of 3% of GSDP on Kerala. Ananthasayanam Ayyangar proposed a Finance Commission-like body to strengthen fiscal federalism by balancing fiscal discipline with State autonomy.


Feature
	Symmetric Federalism
	Asymmetric Federalism
	Definition
	All states have equal status and powers under the Constitution.
	Some states/regions have special privileges or different powers.
	Consti Basis for states
	Part XI (Articles 245-263) - Defines Union-State relations.
	Articles 370, 371, 239AA, Fifth & Sixth Schedules 
	Legislative Powers
	Articles 245-255 - Parliament and state legislatures have defined powers (Seventh Schedule: Union, State, Concurrent Lists).
	Articles 370 (J&K - revoked in 2019)
Article 371 to 371J (special provisions for various states).
	Executive Powers
	Articles 256-263 - Uniform executive structure across states.
	Article 239A (Puducherry), Article 239AA (Delhi’s special status) - Different governance models.
	Financial Relations
	Articles 268-281 - Taxation, grants, Finance Commission recommendations apply uniformly.
	Article 275 (Statutory Grants-in-aid), Article 280 (Special financial provisions for certain states).
	Union Territories
	Governed by the Union, with centrally appointed administrators.
	Some UTs like Delhi and Pondicherry have legislatures with special powers.
	Scheduled & Tribal Areas
	

	Fifth Schedule (Scheduled Areas)
Sixth Schedule (Autonomous Councils in North-East) - Provide autonomy to tribal regions.
	

Evolution of Cooperative Federalism in India
India’s federalism has evolved from rigid power demarcation to greater collaboration between the Union and States.
1. Historical Foundations
                                                         * Simon Commission Report (1927) → Recommended provincial autonomy.
                                                         * GoI Act, 1935 → Introduced federal structure with Union, Provincial, and Concurrent Lists.
2. Institutional Framework
                                                         * Inter-State Council (A.263, 1990) → Enhances Union-State coordination.
                                                         * Zonal Councils (State Reorg. Act, 1956) → Regional cooperation platforms.
                                                         * National Development Council (1952, now NITI Aayog) → Strengthened planning collaboration.
                                                         * National Integration Council (1961) → Promotes national unity.
3. Constitutional Provisions
                                                         * Legislative Powers (A.246, 7th Schedule) → Union, State, Concurrent Lists.
                                                         * All India Services (A.312) → Common cadre ensures administrative uniformity.
                                                         * Integrated Judiciary → Enforces both Union and State laws.
                                                         * Full Faith & Credit Clause (A.261) → Legal recognition of state laws nationwide.
                                                         * Financial Coordination:
                                                         * Finance Commission (A.280) → Revenue distribution.
                                                         * GST Council (A.279A, 2017) → Fiscal cooperation in taxation.
4. Maturing Democracy
                                                         * President’s Rule (A.356): 92 instances (1950-1991) → Reduced to 32 (1991-2016), reflecting democratic maturity (S.R. Bommai case, 1994).
Conclusion- India’s federalism has transitioned from rigid central control to cooperative mechanisms, ensuring better fiscal, administrative, and legislative harmony.
	



Inter-state relations 
Provision in constitution regarding interstate harmony 
                                                         * Inter state water disputes
                                                         * A 262 provides its adjudication
                                                         * Parliament by law adjudicate wrt Use, Distribution, Control of water of  inter state river, valley
                                                         * Neither SC, nor other court can tender jurisdiction in any dispute. 
                                                         * Entry 56 of the union list deals with regulation, dev of inter state river/ valleys.- declared by parliament by law in pub interest. 
                                                         * Entry 17 of  state list- water supply, irrigation, canal, drainage, water storage, Hydro power etc
                                                         * Parliamentary laws in this regard -
                                                         * River board act 1956=  estb river board for development & regulating inter state river
                                                         * No such board estb so far. 
                                                         * Inter state water disputes act 1956
                                                         * Central govt has power to set an ad hoc tribunal on request of state govt if convinced that water disputes  are not solvable by negotiations. Hence the state can't approach directly SC
                                                         * Decision is final & binding-  Fixed Time period for tribunal 3 years, exceptional case = 5 yrs.
                                                         * 1st tribunal- Krishna
                                                         * No court jurisdiction-
                                                         * State can still approach SC under A 136 (special leave)
                                                         * Private persons could approach SC under violation of Article 21 (Right to Life)
                                                         * Chairman & member of water dispute tribunals are CJI nominated.


                                                         * Inter state council (article 263)
                                                         * Art 263 estd ISC facilitate coordination between state-centre- Non permanent body
                                                         * 1st recommended by 1st ARC (1966)
                                                         * Sarkaria commission (1983-88) recommendation lead to finally establishing by Janta dal govt (VP singh) in 1990
                                                         * Last meeting 2017 under modi
                                                         * President by order estd and also defines the nature of duty of council. 
                                                         * Enquire, advise on Legal/ non legal interstate disputes
                                                         * Complementary to SC jurisdiction under A 131
                                                         * Must meet thrice a year
                                                         * Composition
                                                         * PM - chairman
                                                         * CM of all states/ UT with legislative assemblies 
                                                         * Administrators of UT not having legislative assemblies
                                                         * Governor of state under prez rule
                                                         * 6 central cabinet minister incl home min - PM nominate
                                                         * 5 cabinet ministers/ minister of state - PM nominated = permanent invitees.
                                                         * Standing committee
                                                         * Chairman = union home min
                                                         * 5 union cabinet min
                                                         * 9 CM
                                                         * Council assisted by Inter state council secretariat estd 1991, headed by GoI secretary 
3. Public Acts, record and judicial proceeding.
The Full Faith and Credit Clause ensures recognition of acts, records, and judicial proceedings across states. Key points:
                                                         1. Applies to: Public acts (legislative + executive), records, and judicial proceedings of Centre & States.
                                                         2. Regulation: Parliament decides the manner and effect of such recognition.
                                                         3. “Civil” Court Judgments: (not criminal)- Enforceable across India.


4. Inter state trade & comm ( A 301 - 307- part 13)
                                                         * Trade, commerce, intercourse throughout india shall be free
                                                         * Parliament can curtail freedom of trade in public interest but parliament can give preference to others except in case of scarcity of goods. 
                                                         * State legislature can impose restrictions or tax in inter/ intra state in public interest. Such a bill can only Prez's previous sanction. But it can't be discriminatory between states. 
                                                         * Parliament can appoint apt auth for abv purposes. Parliament can also confer necessary powers & duties on this authority. 
  
Zonal councils 
                                                         * Statutory bodies estd under State Reorganisation Act of 1956.
                                                         * 5 zones - 
                                                         * Northern- HQ - New Delhi. Ladakh, J&K, Himachal, Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan, Chandigarh, Delhi 
                                                         * Central- HQ- Allahabad. Uttarakhand, UP, MP, chhattisgarh
                                                         * Eastern- HQ- Calcutta. Bihar, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha
                                                         * Western - HQ - Mumbai. Gujarat, MH, Goa, dadra nagar haweli, Daman & Diu
                                                         * Southern- HQ - chennai. Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Kerala and Puducherry 
                                                         * Composition 
                                                         * Central Home minister 
                                                         * CM + 2 ministers of all states in zone 
                                                         * Each CM acts as vice-chair by rotation (1 year)
                                                         * Administrator of each UT in zone 
                                                         * Objective - emotional integration , arresting regionalism
                                                         * North-East council 
                                                         * Statutory body ( NE council act 1971) 
                                                         * 8 sisters
                                                         * In addition to function of zonal council, it has to formula unified & coordinated regional plan

	Scheduled areas (article 244 part 10 )- to provide self governance and preserve tribal autonomy
                                                            * 5th schedule {article 244(1)} - Scheduled Areas & ST in 5th schedule
                                                            * Role of President
                                                            * Declares scheduled area & can (with governor’s consultation) increase, decrease area (Parlia increase/ decrease is for Sch tribes)
                                                            * Appoint commission to report on admin at any time, but compulsorily after 10 years  of commencement of Constitution.
                                                            * The executive power of the State extends to Scheduled Areas, but the Centre can give directions to the State regarding their admin.
                                                            * Role of Governor
                                                            * Decides Laws (parliament & state) applicable
                                                            * Report to Prez regarding administration- annually/ when reqd
                                                            * Making of regulation- by Governor to repeal or amend any act of parlia or SLA on advice of TAC. Assent of president reqd if it amends or repeals parliament laws
                                                            * Tribes “advisory” council- estd by state
                                                            * 20 members, 3/4th members (15) to be ST rep in SLA
                                                            * Similar council can be estd in non-scheduled areas with president’s direction
                                                            * Lesser autonomy than adcs as TAC is just an advisory body to the governor.
                                                            * 5th schedule states- 10 states
                                                            * North- HP
                                                            * South – Andhra Pradesh, Telangana
                                                            * East – Jharkhand, Odisha
                                                            * West – Rajasthan, Gujarat, Maharashtra
                                                            * Centre- Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh


PVTGs are more vulnerable tribals, identified after the Dhebar Commission.
                                                            * Criteria
                                                            * Pre-agricultural technology
                                                            *  Extremely low literacy
                                                            * Stagnant/declining population
                                                            * Subsistence-based economy or Forest-dependent livelihood
                                                            * 75 PVTGs in 18 States/UTs (Odisha has the most – 13 PVTGs).
                                                            * Identified by Tribal Research Institutes (TRIs) by 1993-94; declared by MoTA  via executive action
                                                            * FRA 2006 recognizes their forest & habitat rights, including community tenure.
                                                            * Baigas (MP)- first PVTG to receive habitat rights (not exclusive rights) in India (total 10 so far) (given by state govt)
                                                            * While not in the Constitution, they are a subset of Scheduled Tribes; thus, only Parliament can remove them from the ST list.
	

                                                            * 6th schedule {article 244(2)} - Tribal Areas 
                                                            * Laws applicable- 
                                                            * President for central laws for MTM
                                                            * Governor for states laws for AMTM and central laws assam.
                                                            * Governor can appoint a commission to report on ADCs & he may dissolve ADC or ARC on recommendation of this commission. 
                                                            * ADCs and ARCs
                                                            * Don't fall outside of state executive authority
                                                            * Governor declares/ increase/ decrease area
                                                            * Each ADC has 30 members- 26 elected on basis of UAF hold office for 5 years, 4 nominated by governor hold office under governor’s pleasure
                                                            * Each autonomous region has separate ADC 
                                                            * Legislative-Making of laws- ADCs with consent of the governor on matters like land, forest, agriculture, water, inheritance, marriage, divorce, village admin, regulations on money lending and trading by non-tribals.
                                                            * Judicial- Can constitute courts for trials (village councils). Jurisdiction of HC over these suits is specified by the governor.
                                                            * Executive- estd primary schools, markets, ferries, fisheries, roads , fisheries
                                                            * Revenue- assess and collect land revenue and other taxes
                                                            * Applicable on
                                                            * 3 in Assam- North cachar, Karbi Anglong, Bodoland
                                                            * Tripura-  tripura tribal
                                                            * 3 in Meghalaya- garo, khasi, jaintia
                                                            * 3 in Mizoram- Chakma, Mara, Lai





Emergency provisions- Part 18 (Article 352-360)
                                                               * Converts federal structure to unitary one without formal amendment to constitution. 
	Aspect
	Proclamation of Emergency (Art. 352); Aka National Emergency 
	President's Rule  (Art. 356)
(Emergency word is not used )
	Financial Emergency 
(Art. 360)

                                                               * Armed rebellion (44th CAA replaced “internal disturbance”)

Art 365- failure to comply with Union’s direction

(Earlier: 2 month, Simple majority)

	If the Lok Sabha is dissolved, it survives for 30 days from the first sitting of the new Lok Sabha.

	Effects on Centre-State Relations
	Centre gets concurrent power on state matters. Under this relationship the centre with all states are modified.
Executive: Centre (Prez, CoM, PM) gets power to direct any state on any matter. 
State govt under complete central control, though not suspended. 
	State matters are assumed by the centre. Centre’s relation with only specific states is modified.
Executive: President assumes state’s executive powers; Governor rules in the name of the President with help of Chief Secretary of State
CoM (headed by CM) dismissed  by President.
	Executive: President can issue financial directives to states to reduce salary, allowance of SC/ HC judge
	Legislative: Normal distribution of legis powers b/w centre and state is suspended but state legislature is not suspended.
                                                               1. Parliament can make laws on State List subjects or extend central laws to the state.
                                                               2. Parliament cannot delegate legis functions to any other authority.
                                                               3. States can still make laws subject to overriding powers of parliament.
                                                               4. Laws coterminous with emergency- cease after 6 months from cessation.
                                                               5. The President can issue ordinances on state subjects.
                                                               6. The 42nd CAA allowed Union executive and legislative powers to extend to any state during a National Emergency, while the 44th CAA restricted this automatic extension.
	Legislative: 
                                                               * Parliament assumes legislative functions of the state.
                                                               * Parliament delegates legislative power to Prez or any authority.
                                                               * SL can only be suspended by the president but dissolved only at the instance of Parliament approval.
                                                               * Laws NOT coterminous


	

	Financial: President with approval of parliament can alter distribution of financial resources between Centre and States.
Lasts on till emergency lasts
	Financial: No specific financial impact. If Lok Sabha isn’t in session, the President can authorize expenditure from the state's Consolidated Fund, with parliament’s approval.
	Financial: President with approval of parliament gains full control over state financial matters; salaries of government officials and judges can be reduced.
	Effects on Lok Sabha
	Can be extended beyond its normal 5-year term for up to 1 year at a time (max 6 months after emergency ends).
	No impact.
	No impact.
	Effects on FRs
	Art. 358: Automatically suspends Art. 19(only in case of war or external aggression).
	No.
	No.
	Art. 359: President can suspend enforcement of other FRs (except Art. 20 & 21) through a separate order.
	

	

	Distinction between Art. 358 & 359
	Article 358: Applies only to Art. 19, effective immediately, applies to the entire country & entire duration of emergency, and laws violating Art. 19 cannot be challenged.
	Not applicable.
	Not applicable.
	Article 359: Applies to any Fundamental Right (except Art. 20 & 21), requires a Presidential order, applies to specific regions or the whole country, and suspends only remedies, not the right itself.
	

	

	Revocation
	Can be revoked by the President at any time without parliamentary approval
	Same 
	Same
	Can also be revoked if 1/10th of Lok Sabha members give a written notice to the Speaker/ President to hold a special sitting within 14 days- simple majority (44th CAA)
	

	

	Scope of Judicial Review (JR)
	44th Amendment: Emergency can be challenged in court if based on mala fide intentions (irrelevance/ absurdity).
	JR allowed in case of improper use, malafide. If misused, courts can reinstate dismissed state governments.
(S.R. Bommai case, 1994). 
	JR allowed but rarely invoked due to economic nature.
	Cases of Proper Use
	1962 (China War), 1971 (Indo-Pak War)- external
	1st time implemented in PEPSU
not implemented till yet in Telangana, Chhattisgarh. 
Most times implemented in Manipur (11 times) 
                                                               1. Hung assembly
                                                               2. majority party declines to form govt and governor cant find a coalition
                                                               3. Ministry resigns and no alternate ministry
                                                               4. Article 356, 365- 
                                                               5. Physical breakdown where govt wilfully refuses to discharge constitutional obligation 
	Never declared yet.
	Cases of Improper Use
	1975 (Indira Gandhi’s Emergency - political misuse).
	1977 (Janata Govt dismissed Congress state govts - struck down in S.R. Bommai case).
BR Ambedkar had said art 356 would remain a dead letter
                                                               1. Maladministration/ corruption or w/o prior warning
                                                               2. Prez rule imposed w/o probing alternative ministry
                                                               3. The governor makes his own assessment without a floor test. 
                                                               4. Internal disturbance not amounting to internal subversion/ physical breakdown.
                                                               5. Misuse of power to sort intra party problems. Ex: 1977 actions of morarji desai and 1980 actions of indira gandhi
	

	

Public Interest Litigation 

Public Interest Litigation (PIL), is a judicial innovation introduced in the 1980s, by Justice V.R. Krishna Iyer and Justice P.N. Bhagwati. A PIL is an extension of writ jurisdiction  filed in the SC (Article 32), HC (Article 226), NGT (for environmental issues under NGT Act, 2010). It led to the relaxation of the traditional locus-standi rule. 


Features/ Purpose/ Principles of PIL- (RuPu NaVu LoCoNo)
                                                               * Relaxed Locus Standi rule – allowing any public-spirited individual to approach the court for enforcing the rights of others, rather than only the aggrieved party.
                                                               * Vindication of Rule of Law–S.P. Gupta v. UoI (1981): 1st PIL- Established PIL as a tool for justice and promoted transparency in judicial appointments.
                                                               * Public Interest First – Public Interest Foundation v. UoI (2018): Mandated candidates to declare their criminal records before elections to curb criminalization of politics.
                                                               * Natural Justice Principle– Overrides technical obligations and prioritising Human rights and FRs 14 and 21. Ex: Olga Tellis v. Bombay MNC (1985) → Protected pavement dwellers' right to livelihood.
                                                               * Justice for the Vulnerable– Hussainara Khatoon Case (1979) → Ensured free legal aid & speedy trial for undertrials; Vishaka judgement (1997) – guidelines against workplace sexual harassment.
                                                               * Collective Action – Bandhua Mukti Morcha v. UoI (1984) → SC intervened to abolish bonded labor.
                                                               * Non-Adversarial– M.C. Mehta v. UoI (1986) → Led to stricter environmental laws; Taj Mahal pollution, Ganga water pollution cases.
Scope of PIL
                                                               * Vulnerable Sections
                                                               * SC/ST 
                                                               * Women's Rights & Safety
                                                               * Neglected Children 
                                                               * Labour Rights & Workplace Justice
                                                               * Bonded Labour
                                                               * Casual Worker Exploitation- Non-payment of Minimum Wage 
                                                               * Prisoner & Police Accountability
                                                               * Petitions from Jail –harassment, delayed release, and speedy trials.
                                                               * Police Brutality – Non-registration of cases, custodial deaths, and harassment.
                                                               * Social Harmony
                                                               * Riot Victims
                                                               * Villager Harassment- social oppression and torture.
                                                               * Heritage & Culture Preservation – Protection of historical and cultural assets.
                                                               * Family Pension – Ensuring rightful benefits to dependents.
                                                               * Environmental & food
                                                               * Pollution & Ecological Issues 
                                                               * Food & Drug Adulteration
                                                               * Following cases out of scope- 
                                                               * Landlord- tenant matter.
                                                               * Service matters pertaining to pension and gratuity (of individual govt employees)
                                                               * Complaint against CG/SG Dept, local body except abv matters
                                                               * Admission to medical, edu institutions.
                                                               * Petition for early hearing of cases pending in high court and subordinate court.
Way forward- PIL Admission Guidelines by SC
                                                               1. Promote genuine PIL-- should not become publicity, politics, private gain, or middle-class interest litigations.
                                                               2. HCs should frame rules to ensure authenticity on these lines
                                                               1. Verify petitioner’s credentials & substantial public interest.
                                                               2. Prioritize PILs addressing larger public concerns.
                                                               3. Penalize frivolous petitions with exemplary costs.
                                                               3. Jurisdiction Limits – SC held that PIL cannot challenge a valid statutory rule like service conditions set by law unless it violates constitutional rights.
Conclusion- SC states PIL is not a universal remedy but protects human rights of weaker sections through public-spirited petitions.

Category
	Details

	73rd CAA, 9th part, 11th Schedule; Article 40 of DPSP; Local governance in Schedule 7- state list.

	Evolution of Panchayati Raj


(BAG SiGaT)
	

Committee
	Year
	Structure 
	Unique Recommendations
	Balwant Rai Mehta
	1957
	3-tier: 
1. Gram Panchayat (Village) 
2. Panchayat Samiti (Block) 
3. Zila Parishad (District)
	- Direct elections at the village level; indirect elections for higher tiers. 
- Panchayat Samiti as the executive body
- Zila Parishad as advisory and supervisory. 
- District Collector as chairman of Zila Parishad. 
	Ashok Mehta
	1977
	2-tier: 
1. Zila Parishad (District) 
2. Mandal Panchayat 
(Group of villages)
	- Zila Parishad as the executive body responsible for district-level planning.
- Participation of political parties in elections. 
- Reservation for (SCs) and (STs). 
- Compulsory taxation powers for PRIs. (wanted to reduce DC role) 
	GVK Rao
	1985
	Recommended reducing the burden of the DC in development administration. 
	- Criticized excessive bureaucratization, terming it "grass without roots". 
 - Zila Parishad as the key body for democratic decentralization- to plan, implement, monitor rural deve
- It proposed creating a new post called the District Development Commissioner (DDC) to serve as the Chief Executive Officer of the Zila Parishad, specifically so that developmental functions would be handled by a technical expert separate from the DC’s traditional regulatory/ revenue roles.
	LM Singhvi
	1986
	Focused on revitalizing PRIs
	- Advocated for constitutional recognition of PRIs (1st time ever)
- Estd of Nyaya Panchayats for clusters of villages. 
- Enhanced financial resources for village Panchayats. 
	Gadgil
	1988
	3-tier with direct elections at all levels.
	- Recommended constitutional status for PRIs. 
- Establishment of State Finance Com , State Election Com. 
- Empowered PRIs to levy, collect, and use taxes.
	Thungon
	1989
	3-tier: 
1. Village Panchayat
2. Block Panchayat
3. Zila Parishad
	- Parliamentary sub committee
- Zila Parishad as the sole central planning and development body with DC as the Coordinator of the Zila Parishad
- Fixed tenure of 5 years for PRIs; super session limit of 6 months. 
- Reservation for SCs, STs, and women at all tiers. 
	The Hanumantha Rao Committee (1984) actually favored the District Collector's dominance. It recommended:
                                                               * Collector as Leader: The District Collector (or a Minister) should head the district planning body.
                                                               * Decentralized Planning: Planning should happen at the district level, but under bureaucratic control rather than purely through elected PRIs.
                                                               * Technical Focus: It emphasized "separate" planning bodies to ensure technical and economic efficiency, rather than political devolution.
	Constitution-alization of PRI
	                                                               1. Rajiv Gandhi Govt (1989)- 64th CAB- 1989 for constitutionalizing PRI. Passed in Lok Sabha but rejected in Rajya Sabha.
                                                               2. V P Singh 1990- Intro new CAB in 1990 but lapsed due to government fall
                                                               3. Narasimha Rao Govt (1991-92)- Reviewed & modified proposals, removing controversial provisions
                                                               4. Passed as 73rd CAA (1992), effective 24 April 1993

	243A

Members of panchayat @ all 3 levels elected directly by people

Newly elected panchayat holds only for remaining period; elections not required if remainder term <6 months

the State Election Commissioner (SEC). However, once appointed, the SEC enjoys protection similar to a High Court judge and cannot be removed arbitrarily by the Governor.

	: Bars courts from questioning Panchayat elections (electoral matters like delimitation & allocation of seats),  except through an election petition as prescribed by law.
	State election commission
	                                                               * Appointment: by the Governor
                                                               * Removal: Only same manner & grounds as HC judge, requiring Presidential approval.
                                                               * Powers: Conducts, controls, supervises elections to local bodies (Panchayats & Municipalities) as per Article 243K & 243ZA of Constitution. 
Make provisions w..r.t all matters relating to elections of panchayat.
	Compulsory Provisions
	                                                                  * Gram sabha formation.
                                                                  * 3-tier Panchayati Raj; intermediate optional for states <20 lakh population).
                                                                  * Voting rights for all directly & indirectly elected members.
                                                                  * Direct elections for all seats; indirect elections for chairpersons at intermediate & district levels.
                                                                  * Minimum age: 21 years to contest elections.
                                                                  * Reservation for SCs, STs, and one-third for women (including SC/ST women).
                                                                  * Fixed tenure: 5 years; elections within 6 months of dissolution.
                                                                  * The State Election Commission (SEC) conducts and oversees elections.
                                                                  * The State Finance Commission (SFC) reviews finances every 5 years.
	Voluntary Provisions
	                                                                  * Determining manner of elections of panchayat chairperson
                                                                  * giving rep to chairperson of GP @ intermediate & rep to chairpersons of intermediate level at district level
                                                                  * Representation of MPs/MLAs in panchayats within their constituencies.
                                                                  * Reservation for Backward Classes (BCs) at any level.
