	PESA Act (1996)
	                                                                  * Applicable in 5th Schedule Areas by Parliament 
                                                                  * Dileep Singh Bhuria Com- Recommended extending Part IX to Schedule Areas, self-rule for tribals, administrative framework with traditional practices
                                                                  * Objectives- Empower Panchayats, prevent power centralization by panchayats at higher level, preserve tribal governance.
                                                                  * Features
                                                                  * Customary Law Compliance- by State legislations on panchayats
                                                                  * Reservation- reservation should be min 50% of seats but it can be more in proportion to population. 
                                                                  * All Chairperson seats reserved for STs, 
                                                                  * state government can nominate up to 1/10 STs if rep is inadequate
                                                                  * Gram Sabha-  approve plans for socio- economic deve (actual planning & implementation at state level), identify poverty alleviation beneficiaries, consult on land acquisition
                                                                  * Mandatory approval of GP or GS for granting license for minor minerals 
                                                                  * Ensured Rights- MFP, Certify fund utilization, manage minor water bodies, Prohibition of intoxicants, prevention of land alienation, regulation of money lending to STs, resource planning
                                                                  * State legislatures should model district-level Panchayat administration in Scheduled Areas on the Sixth Schedule
                                                                  * Any conflicting law on Panchayats in Scheduled Areas will become invalid one year after this Act gets presidential assent.
                                                                  * Existing Panchayats will continue until their term ends unless dissolved earlier by the State Legislature.
	Municipal Corp  
Category
	Details
	Functions
	18 functions (12 schedule)-
1. Urban Planning & Deve- Urban planning, land use regulation, economic & social deve
2. Infra & Services- Roads, bridges, water supply, sanitation, solid waste, fire services
3. Welfare & Social Justice- Slum improvement, poverty alleviation, welfare of weaker sections
4. Municipal Amenities & Governance- Parks, playgrounds, cultural & educational aspects, burial grounds, animal care
5. Commerce & Revenue- Slaughterhouses, tanneries, birth & death records, street lighting, public amenities
	Consti Provisions
	- 12th Schedule, Part 9A, Articles 243P to 243ZG.
	Types of ULBs
	- Municipal Corp, Municipality, Notified Area Committee, Town Area Committee, Cantonment Board, Township, Port Trust, Special Purpose Agency.
	Administrative Authority
	- Ministry of Urban Development (General).
- Ministry of Defence (Cantonment).
- Ministry of Home Affairs (Union Territories).
	Historical Evolution
	- 1687: First Municipal Corporation (Madras).
- 1726: Corporations in Bombay & Calcutta. 
- 1870: Lord Mayo’s Resolution (Financial decentralization).
- 1882: Lord Ripon’s Resolution (Father of Local Self-Government).
- 1909: Royal Commission on Decentralization (Chairman: Hobhouse).
- 1919 GOI Act: Made it a transferred subject.
- 1935 GOI Act: Local self-government became a provincial subject.
	Constitutionalization
	- 1989: 65th Amendment Bill (Nagar Palika Bill) by Rajiv Gandhi (Defeated in Rajya Sabha).
- 1991: Modified by Narasimha Rao Govt, leading to 74th CAA, 1992.
	Metropolitan Area (243P(c))
	- Population ≥10 lakhs, covering one or more districts with two or more municipalities/  panchayats, notified by the Governor. 
	Definition of Urban Area
	- Min population: 5000.
- 75% male workforce in the non-agriculture sector.
- Population density >400 per sq. km.
	Structure of Urban Local Bodies (243Q)
	- Nagar Panchayat (Transitional Area).
- Municipal Council (Small Urban Area)
- Municipal Corporation (Large Urban Area).
- Metropolitan Development Authority (Planning for the entire metro region).
	Composition & Elections (243R)
	- Direct elections.
- Municipality divided into wards.
- Legislature decides election procedure. (The Constitution provides the requirement and authority for elections, while the State Legislature provides the detailed procedural rules and machinery. )
- Special representation for experts, MPs, MLAs, chairpersons (w/o voting rights).
	Ward Committee (243S)
	- Mandatory for cities with a population of 3+ lakhs.
- The State Legislature can create additional committees.
	Reservation (243T)
	- Same as Panchayati Raj (73rd Amendment).
	Duration (243U)
	- 5-year term, same as Panchayati Raj.
	Disqualification (243V)
	- Same as State Legislature election laws.
- Minimum age: 21 years.
	Powers & Functions (243W)
	- State can devolve powers.


	Financial Powers (243X)
	- State Legislature specifies taxation powers.
- Municipalities collect taxes, duties, fees.
- State shares revenue with municipalities.
- Grants-in-aid from State.
	Finance Commission (243Y)
	- Reviews financial position of municipalities.
- Recommends:
         1. Revenue-sharing between State & Municipality.
         2. Assigned taxes & duties.
         3. Measures to improve finances.
- The Governor places recommendations before the Legislature.
	Audit & Accounts (243Z)
	- State Legislature ensures financial accountability.
	State Election Commission (243ZA)
	- Supervises municipal elections, prepares electoral rolls.
- Removal only on grounds similar to HC judges.
	Application to UTs (243ZB)
	- President decides applicability to UTs.
	Exempted Areas (243ZC)
	Same as PRI
	District Planning Committee (243ZD)
	-mandatory in every district 
State constitutes a committee at the district level to prepare a consolidated draft development plan.
- 4/5th of members elected by elected members of District Panchayats & Municipalities from among themselves (indirect election), rest are nominated by state govt 
- Representation proportional to rural & urban population.
	

Topic
	Details
	Metropolitan Planning Committee (MPC) - Article 243ZE
	- Constituted in each metropolitan area. 
- Prepare a draft development plan. 
- Legislature decides composition, elections, and functions. 
- Composition: ⅔ members indirectly elected by municipalities and panchayats to ensure democratic representation. . 
- Issues: Only 9 out of 18 mandated cities have MPCs, limited functionality, minimal local rep.
	Article 243ZF (Existing Laws & Municipalities)
	- Pre-existing municipal laws continue unless repealed or amended within a year. 
- Municipalities before the 74th Amendment continue until dissolved by legislature. 
- Article 243ZG: Courts cannot interfere in municipal elections.
	Types of Urban Government
	Municipal Corporation (Big cities) 
- Established by the State Legislature (State) / Parliament (UTs). 
- 3 Authorities: 
- Council (elected)
- Mayor: Direct/ Indirectly elected, ceremonial head, presides over meetings. 
- Municipal Commissioner: CEO, (appointed) by State Govt (IAS). 
- Standing Committee: Manages education, health, taxation, finance.
	Municipality 
(Small cities/towns)
	- Similar structure as Municipal Corporation. 
- Headed by a President/Chairman (more executive role than Mayor). 
- CEO (IAS) appointed by State Govt.
	Notified Area Committee
	- For rapidly developing towns without municipal status. 
- Established via government notification. 
- Entirely nominated body, not statutory.
	Town Area Committee
	- For small towns with limited functions (drainage, roads, lighting). 
- Governed by a separate State Law. 
- Can be wholly/partially elected or nominated.
	Cantonment Board
	- Administers civil population in cantonment areas (Under Defence Ministry). 
- Governed by Cantonment Act, 2006. 
- Partly elected (5-year term), partly nominated. 
- Military Officer Commanding = ex-officio President 
- CEO implements board decisions (IDES)
	Township
	- Created by public enterprises for housing staff/workers (ex mining colonies). 
- No elected members, run by bureaucrats.
- No statutory
	Port Trust
	- Manages ports & provides civic amenities in port areas (Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai) 
- Created by Parliament Act. 
- Consists of elected & nominated members.
	Special Purpose Agencies
	- Autonomous bodies for specific urban functions. 
- Established via legislative acts or executive resolutions. 
- Independent, not subordinate to municipal bodies.
	Municipal Personnel Systems 
	- Separate System: Local bodies appoint/manage their own staff (most common). 
- Unified System: State govt appoints & transfers personnel (e.g., UP, TN, MP, AP). 
- Integrated System: No distinction between state & municipal services (e.g., Bihar, Odisha, Karnataka).
                                                                  * Compulsory and voluntary provisions- same as PRIs
	Municipal Revenue
	Tax Revenue- Property tax (main revenue source), General tax, Water tax. 
Non-Tax - Rent from municipal properties, fees, charges (water, sanitation). 
Grants & Devolution- Funds from State Govt via State Finance Commission. 
Loans - From state/financial institutions (with govt approval).
	Central Council of Local Government
	- Estd in 1954 under Article 263 by presidential order (not directly by constitution)- non constitutional body
- Union Minister of Urban Dev is Chairman. 
- Functions: Policy recommendations, legislative proposals, financial reviews, coordination between Centre & States, reviewing local governance.
	


 Constitutional bodies 
                                                                  * Election commission
                                                                  * A 324 - power of  superintendence, direction , control of following is vested in EC ( all india body)
                                                                  * Electoral rolls prep
                                                                  * Election of prez, VP, Parliament, SLA
                                                                  * Vision - Aims to be an excellence hub by promoting participation and strengthening electoral democracy in India and globally.
                                                                  * Assistance to EC
                                                                  * Deputy Election Commissioners (IAS): Appointed by ECI at central level; help in conducting elections smoothly (e.g., overseeing voter registration).
                                                                  * Secretariat Officials (Various Ranks): Appointed by the ECI at central level; support the commission with administrative tasks (e.g., managing election records).
                                                                  * Chief Electoral Officer (IAS): Appointed by the ECI in consultation with the state government; supervises elections in the state (e.g., ensuring fair polling across districts).
                                                                  * District Collector / District Returning Officer (IAS): Appointed by the ECI; appoints Returning Officers for managing elections in constituencies  (e.g., selecting polling booth officers).
                                                                  * Presiding Officers (Government Officials/Teachers, etc.): polling booth staff- Appointed by the District Returning Officer; manage polling booths and ensure smooth voting (e.g., verifying voter identity and overseeing the ballot process).
                                                                  * Appointment
                                                                  * The CEC (rank 9A) and Other EC (Appointment, Conditions of Service and Term of Office) Act, 2023 introduced key changes:
                                                                  * Appointment Process: The President appoints. A Selection Committee (PM, LOP in LS, and a Union Minister) now appoints the CEC and ECs.
                                                                  * Advice is binding on Prez & decisions taken by ⅔ majority. 
                                                                  * Service Conditions: Their salary is now equal to the Cabinet Secretary’s (rank 11), instead of a Supreme Court Judge’s (rank 9).
                                                                  * Tenure: Fixed six-year term/ 65 years with no reappointment for any govt job both CEC and ECs. EC can become CEC only if he has served less than 6 years and is below 65. 
                                                                  * Security of Tenure- CEC has it but other ECs don't as CEC cannot be removed from his office except in the same manner and on the same grounds as a judge of the Supreme Court. Any other election commissioner or a regional commissioner cannot be removed from office except on the recommendation of the chief election commissioner to president.
                                                                  * Composition
                                                                  * Initially single-member
                                                                  * Made multi-member in 1989 (as voting age was lowered)
                                                                  * Returned to single-member in 1990, again multi-member in 1993
                                                                  * Currently 1+2 members.
                                                                  * Chief Election Commissioner (CEC)- Once appointed, not answerable to govt/ ruling party. Can't appointed to office of profit under states post retirement
                                                                  * Other Election Commissioners- (Number decided by President)
                                                                  * CEC acts as chairman of Election commision when other ECs are appointed
                                                                  * Regional Commissioners (if needed)
                                                                  * CEC & ECs have equal power (Decision by majority vote in case of conflict)
                                                                  * Independence (Article 324)
                                                                  * CEC's Security: removed only on same grounds as SC judge—Prez removes on grounds of proven misbehavior/incapacity (Undefined)
                                                                  * Service Conditions: Cannot be altered to CEC’s disadvantage after appointment.
                                                                  * EC & Regional Commissioners: Not Removable except only on CEC's recommendation.
                                                                  * Flaws in Independence:
                                                                  * No prescribed qualifications.
                                                                  * They can be appointed to “other” government positions after retirement, which has raised concerns about potential political influence. They cannot be reappointed as CEC, but there is no explicit constitutional/ statutory prohibition against them being appointed to other government roles
                                                                  * Powers, Functions of EC
                                                                  * Administrative Powers
                                                                  * Delimitation: Determines territorial areas of electoral constituencies (Delimitation Commission Act of parlia)
                                                                  * The Delimitation Commission redraws parliamentary and state assembly constituencies based on the latest Census (2001) for fair representation.
                                                                  * Composition:
                                                                  * Chairman: Sitting or retired SC judge.
                                                                  * Ex-officio members: CEC or an EC & State Election Commissioners of concerned states.
                                                                  * Electoral Rolls: Prepares and revises
                                                                  * Election Conduct: Notifies election dates, scrutinizes nominations, and supervises the election process.
                                                                  * Code of Conduct: Sets rules for parties and candidates
                                                                  * Election Machinery: Appoints officers and requests staff for election duties.
                                                                  * Publicity Regulation: Prepares rosters for political parties’ radio/TV broadcasts.
                                                                  * Advisory Powers- 
                                                                  * Disqualifications: Advises the President ( for MPs) and Governor ( for MLAs)
                                                                  * Emergency Elections: Advises the President on holding elections in states under President’s Rule.
                                                                  * Quasi-Judicial Powers- 
                                                                  * Party Recognition: Grants recognition, allots symbols.
                                                                  * Acts as court to Settle disputes related to party recognition & symbol allotment.
                                                                  *  Budget & expenditure of ECI
                                                                  * Separate secretariat @ Delhi
                                                                  * Budget prep by Fin min + EC, former accepts recommendation usually
                                                                  * If election =
                                                                  * Parliament, expenditure = CG
                                                                  * SLA = SG
                                                                  * Simultaneous = both
                                                                  * Election Expenditure 
                                                                  * LS Candidate Limit: ₹70–95 lakh (earlier ₹54–70 lakh)
                                                                  * Covers: Meetings, rallies, ads, hoardings, materials, etc.
                                                                  * Counted from nomination of each candidate till result
                                                                  * Star Campaigner- Can carry up to ₹1 lakh. Their travel expenses not added to candidate’s expenditure
                                                                  * Limits -  Max ₹50,000 cash + ₹10,000 material in vehicles. If more than this, can be confiscated
                                                                  * The MCC is a non-codified set of guidelines issued by the ECI to regulate political parties and candidates before elections. It was first introduced in 1960 (Kerala) and adopted nationally in 1968. It comes into effect from the announcement of elections until the completion of the process








	UPSC (ART 315-323, Part 14th)
	SPSC (ART 315-323, Part 14th)
	Composition
	                                                                  * Chairman + 9-11 members
                                                                  * Prez appoint/ remove; decides strength of commission
                                                                  * Office for 6/65 years whichever is earlier. 
                                                                  * President can appoint one of members of UPSC as acting chairman- 
                                                                  * Vacancy in chairman’s office
                                                                  * Absence
                                                                  * Inability to perform duties.
                                                                  * The Acting Chairman continues till the new Chairman assumes office or incumbent resumes duties.
	Same as UPSC except- 
                                                                  * Appointed by the Governor.
                                                                  * strength—Governor decides.
                                                                  * Term: 6 / 62, whichever is earlier
                                                                  * Resign- Governor.
                                                                  * Acting Chairman appointed by Governor if:
	Qualifications
	                                                                  * No qualification in consti except At Least 50% members held Office of GoI, SG ( at least 10 years)
                                                                  * Constitution authorise President to determine Service condition, tenure of chairman & other members (not parlia)
	Same except the Governor determines service conditions.
	Removal (Article 317)
	Can be removed even before expiry of term by President if- (MIME) 
                                                                  * Paid employment outside govt
                                                                  * Adjudged Insolvent,
                                                                  * Infirmity of Mind - Prez's opinion
                                                                  * Misbehaviour- Chairman/ Any member can be removed. But the President has to refer to SC for enquiry & advice tendered is Binding as per consti. Prez can suspend chairman during enquiry. 
                                                                  * Grounds defined in Constitution- 
                                                                  * Interested in govt contracts / agreement
                                                                  * Participates/ Profits in manner other than as minister
                                                                  * Article 317(1) covers removal for misbehaviour (requiring an SC inquiry), and Article 319 generally bars all removed members from future government posts
	                                                                  * Appointed by Governor, removed by President (not Governor).
                                                                  * Removed like UPSC Chairman/member:
                                                                  * For misbehavior, the President refers the case to the Supreme Court for enquiry.
                                                                  * Same as UPSC
	Independence (consti provisions)
	                                                                  * Removal → Only by President as per Constitution (security of tenure).
                                                                  * Service Conditions → Fixed after appointment (Prez can't alter disadvantageously).
                                                                  * Expenses → Salaries, allowances, pensions charged on CFI (No Parliament vote).
                                                                  * Chairman (Post-Tenure completion) → No govt. job after tenure.
                                                                  * Member (Post-Tenure) → Can become UPSC/SPSC Chairman, but no other govt under CG, SG
                                                                  * No 2nd term in same office as UPSC Chairman/Member after 1st term completed
	Same provisions except -
                                                                  * Salaries, allowances & pensions are charged on the Consolidated Fund of the state (not subject to legislature’s vote).
                                                                  * Member / Chairman (post-retirement):
                                                                  * Eligible for UPSC/SPSC Chairman but no other govt. employment.
	Powers/ functions 
(Article 320)


	                                                                  * Consulted on personnel management matters
                                                                  * 1. Recruitment & Appointment, promotions, transfers.
                                                                  * Not binding, but the government/ ministries/ depts cannot reject on its own and must seek ACC (appointment committee of cabinet) approval.
                                                                  * 2. Disciplinary Matters
                                                                  * Not binding, but if the government disregards UPSC’s advice, it must record reasons for doing so.
                                                                  * 3. Service Benefits & Claims
                                                                  * Legal cost reimbursement in official cases.
                                                                  * Pension claims
                                                                  * 4. Temporary & Post-Retirement Matters
                                                                  * Approvals for: Temporary posts more than 1 year.
                                                                  * Service extensions of retired officers.
                                                                  * Joint recruitment with states 
                                                                  * Serve state needs on governor request with prez approval
                                                                  * Parliament expands jurisdiction.
                                                                  * SC held that UPSC consultation is not mandatory; govt decisions remain valid even if not consulted.
                                                                  * No legal remedy: Affected public servants cannot challenge non-consultation in court.
                                                                  * UPSC selection: Does not guarantee a job; govt has final authority though Must act fairly, without bias or malafide intent.
	Same except following - 
                                                                  * Failure to consult SPSC is not justiciable—Govt. The decision remains valid.
                                                                  * The State Legislature can expand SPSC jurisdiction to include local bodies/public institutions.
                                                                  * Annual report to Governor, placed before State Legislature along with reasons for non-acceptance of SPSC advice
	Matter outside jurisdiction


	                                                                  * Reservations- For BC, SCs & STs in govt jobs.
                                                                  * Appointment
                                                                  * Chairmanship/membership of commissions, tribunals, and top diplomatic posts.
                                                                  * Group C & D services selections.
                                                                  * Temporary Appointment (less than 1 year) UPSC consultation not required.
                                                                  * President’s Power
                                                                  * Can exclude certain posts & services from UPSC's scope.
                                                                  * As per Constitution, prez make regulations for AIS & central services & posts where UPSC is not consulted. But she must lay these before both Parliament houses for at least 14 days.
                                                                  * Parliament can amend or repeal these regulations.
	                                                                  * Governor can exclude certain posts/services from SPSC purview via regulations, subject to State Legislature approval
	Role
	                                                                  * Watchdog of merit system - recruit Group A, B
                                                                  * DoPT handles cadre mgmt, training, pay, service conditions etc, NOT UPSC. 
                                                                  * Hence UPSC is the only central recruiting agency, DopT is the central personnel agency. 
                                                                  * CVC consult for  disciplinary matters
	Same except following 
                                                                  * Emergence of the State Vigilance Commission (SVC) has reduced SPSC’s role in disciplinary matters, but SPSC has constitutional supremacy.
                                                                  * Governor consults SPSC & High Court while framing rules for judicial appointments (except district judges).
	Joint pub service commission
	                                                                  * Provisions in constitution- but JSPSC created When 2 or more SL request , by Parliament (hence statutory body)
                                                                  * HR , PB had JSPSC shortly- the only instance 
                                                                  * Term of chairman = 6 year
                                                                  * Prez  appoint chairman & members
                                                                  * Also determine number of member & service condition 
                                                                  * Removed & resign- Prez
                                                                  * Annual performance Report to each state’s concerned governor

                                                                  * Quasi judicial body
                                                                  * President constitutes every 5 years/ when necessary
                                                                  * Constn describes as "balancing wheel of fiscal fed "
                                                                  * Composition = 1 chairman + 4 members ( prez appointed )
                                                                  * Consti authorised Parlia to determine qualifications, selection manners. Hence parliament specified - 
                                                                  * Chairman- experienced in public affairs
                                                                  * HC judge or qualified to be HC judge
                                                                  * Special finance & accounts knowledge
                                                                  * Wide experience in finance & admin. 
                                                                  * Special eco knowledge
                                                                  * Eligible for re-appointment
                                                                  * Office period specified by Prez by order
                                                                  * Report submitted to prez who lays bfr parliament’s both houses with explanation on action taken.
                                                                  * Recommendations
                                                                  * Tax distribution Bw union/ states, Among states
                                                                  * Principles for grant of revenue to  states out of consolidated funds
                                                                  * Measures to augment CF of states to supplement panchayats/ municipalities on the basis of state FC recommendations. 
                                                                  * Other matters ref by Prez in interest of sound finance
                                                                  * Recommendations advisory in nature, not binding (Article 281)
                                                                  * States cant claim recommended money
                                                                  * PV rajamannar (4th FC chairman)- as FC is quasi-judicial constitutional body, its recommendations should not be turned down unless compelling reasons.

National commission for SC / ST
                                                                  * Originally, Art 338 appointed a special officer for SC & ST to investigate matters relating to constitutional safeguards for SC/ ST & report to Prez on working. 
                                                                  * 65th CAA 1990 estd NCSC under art 338 ( part 16 ), so Constitutional body
                                                                  * Statutory bodies - NCW(1992), NCM(1993), NHRC(1993), NCPCR(2007)
                                                                  * 89th CAA 2003- bifurcated into NCSC (Art 338) & NCST (Art 338A)
                                                                  * SC related activity = MoSJE
                                                                  * ST = MoTA (1999)
                                                                  * 84th- census 1991; 86th CAA- RTE, 87th- Delimitation 2001; 89th CAA- SC vs ST
                                                                  * Separate NCSC came into existence in 2004. 
                                                                  * Composition = chairperson, VC, 3 members ( total = 5 )
                                                                  * Chair = Prez appointed
                                                                  * Tenure & service conditions also Prez Determined
                                                                  * Functions
                                                                  * SC /ST - 
                                                                  * Monitor Safeguards – Investigates & evaluates constitutional and legal protections for SC/STs
                                                                  * Handle Complaints – Inquires into violations of ST rights & safeguards
                                                                  * Advise on Deve– Participates in socio-economic planning & evaluates progress.
                                                                  * Report to President – Submits annual & special reports on safeguard implementation.
                                                                  * Recommend Measures –for better protection, welfare & development.
                                                                  * Other Functions – Performs additional duties assigned by the President for ST welfare. Recommends
                                                                  * Protection
                                                                  * Forest & Resource Rights – Grant ownership of minor forest products, minerals, water to STs.
                                                                  * Land Protection – Prevent alienation
                                                                  * Welfare
                                                                  * Rehabilitation – Improved rehab for tribals displaced by projects
                                                                  * Forest Conservation – Encourage tribal participation in afforestation.
                                                                  * Development & Advancement
                                                                  * Livelihood – Promote sustainable jobs & economic development.
                                                                  * PESA Act – Ensure full implementation of Panchayati Raj in Scheduled Areas.
                                                                  * End Shifting Cultivation – Reduce harmful farming practices for better land & environment
                                                                  * Annual report to Prez who place before parliament
                                                                  * Reasons for non compliance/ non acceptance = MUST in report
                                                                  * Prez fwd any report concerning SG to governor who place in state legislature with reasons of non acceptance
                                                                  * Powers 
                                                                  * Regulates Own Procedure- Can frame its own rules for functioning.
                                                                  * Powers of Civil Court while investigating, inquiry in following matters- 
                                                                  * Summon & examine any person across India & for witnesses
                                                                  * Demand documents for investigation.
                                                                  * Receive evidence via affidavits.
                                                                  * Access public records from courts/offices.
                                                                  * Other powers as given by the President.
                                                                  * CG, SG must consult the Commission on key SC (NCSC) , ST (NCST) policy matters.
                                                                  * NCSC Discharge similar functions with regard to Anglo-Indian safeguards- investigate constitutional & legal matters & report -> prez
National commission of BC ( NCBC )
                                                                  * 102nd CAA , art 338b
                                                                  * Also insert A. 342A , amend A 366
                                                                  * Previously statutory body
                                                                  * Composition = Chairman + VC + 3 members
                                                                  * Prez appoint
                                                                  * Powers ~ NCBC
                                                                  * Issues- Recommendation not binding

                                                                  * May do so in governor consultation

                                                                  * Identification
                                                                  * NCBC for the central OBC list (as per Article 340).
                                                                  * State govts for state OBC lists (subject to judicial review).
                                                                  * President (with Parliament’s approval) for SCs and STs under Articles 341 & 342 respectively.

Comptroller and Auditor General of India (CAG) (A 148-151)
                                                                  * CAG is head of indian audit & Auditor General of India & “Guardian of pub purse”
                                                                  * Most imp officer as per BR Ambedkar & Bulwark of democracy
                                                                  * Others = EC, SC, UPSC
                                                                  * Appointment & term
                                                                  * Prez appoint on PM recommendation
                                                                  * Qualifications not mentioned in Consti, decided by govt’s statutory provisions
                                                                  * Oath - 
                                                                  * True faith & allegiance to Consti 
                                                                  * Uphold sovereignty & integrity of india
                                                                  * Perform duty w/o fear, favour, affection, ill-will 
                                                                  * Uphold consti & laws
                                                                  * Hold office for 6 years/ 65 YO
                                                                  * Resign to President 
                                                                  * Removable by Prez on same grounds (proven misbehaviour or incapacity) & in same manner as a SC judge
                                                                  * Agent of Parliament, only responsible to it
                                                                  * Independence
                                                                  * Security of Tenure
                                                                  * Salary & Conditions – Set by Parliament, equal to an SC judge.
                                                                  * No Disadvantageous Changes 
                                                                  * Control Over Department – Service conditions & administrative powers of audit staff decided by the President in consultation with CAG.
                                                                  * Independent Funding – Office expenses, including salaries & pensions, are charged on the CoI, not subjected to parliamentary vote. 
                                                                  * No Further Office – Cannot take ANY government job after retirement.
                                                                  * Submit audit report to Prez/ governor who place before parliament/ SL
                                                                  * 3 audit report submitted
                                                                  * Finance acc
                                                                  * Appropriation acc
                                                                  * On pub undertakings
                                                                  *  Duties & powers
                                                                  * A 149 authorizes Parliament to describe duty, power wrt union & state acc
                                                                  * So, passed CAG ( duty, power & condition ) service act 1971. Amended 1976 to separate accounting from auditing in CG -
                                                                  * Guide , friend, philosopher of PAC of parliament
                                                                  * Govt accounts- All expenditure from Consolidated fund, contingency fund of India, each state, UT with legislature 
                                                                  * Public account of india 
                                                                  * Departmental Accounts –Trading, manufacturing, profits & loss acc, balance sheets, other subsidiary accounts of CG/ SG dept
                                                                  * Revenue Compliance – Ensures proper assessment, collection, and allocation of taxes & duties
                                                                  * Receipt & expenditure of
                                                                  * Centrally/ state fin body
                                                                  * Govt company
                                                                  * Other corporations & bodies when law need
                                                                  * Debt & Transactions – Audits public debt, sinking funds, deposits, advances, suspense accounts, remittances, stock accounts, etc., with Presidential approval.
                                                                  * Audits any authority upon Presidential/Governor’s request, including local bodies.
                                                                  * Form of Accounts (A150) – Advises the President on the form in which central & state government accounts shall be kept
                                                                  * Audit Reports (A151) – Submits reports to:
                                                                  * President, who presents them to Parliament.
                                                                  * Governor, who presents them to the State Legislature.
                                                                  * Tax & Duty Certification (A279) – Determines net proceeds of any tax/duty; His certificate is final.
                                                                  * Net proceeds =tax duty minus cost of collection
                                                                  * State Accounts Compilation – Earlier maintained state government accounts; relieved of central govt accounting in 1976 due to departmentalisation.
        
Limitations in role
                                                                  * Can't disapprove expenditure unlike Britain - no comptroller role
                                                                  * Though constitution envisage CAG, but only AG role practically
                                                                  * Can’t audit secret service expenditure, only receive certificate
                                                                  * No time frame to submit report
                                                                  * Proprietary audit ( look wisdom, eco of govt expenditure ) = discretionary
                                                                  * Only legal & regulatory audit is obligatory.
                                                                  * Ltd role in auditing pub corp
                                                                  * Some audited totally, directly like ONGC/ damodar valley corp
                                                                  * Some are audited by pvt , appointed by CAG. Ex - central warehousing corp, indus financing corp
                                                                  * Appleby's criticism

                                                                  * Can't accept position as director of company without govt permission

Category
	Languages
	Official Language
	Part XVII (Articles 343-351): Provisions divided into four heads - Language of the Union, Regional Language, Language of Judiciary & Text of Law, Special Directives.
	Language of the Union
	Article 343: Hindi in Devanagari script is the official language. + English (parlia law)
Numerals: International form, not Devanagari. 
Commission on Official Language: Appointed by the President to promote Hindi and restrict English. 
Parliamentary Committee on Official Language reviews progress (B.G. Kher Committee in 1955).
	Regional Languages
	Article 345: States can adopt one or more official languages; otherwise, English remains official. 
Examples: Andhra Pradesh (Telugu), Kerala (Malayalam), Odisha (Odia), Gujarat (Gujarati & Hindi), Goa (Marathi & Konkani), Meghalaya, Arunachal, Nagaland (English). 
Inter-state Communication: Hindi between Hindi states; English between Union and non-Hindi states. Hindi communication with non-Hindi states must have an English translation. 
Article 347: The President can recognize a language if a substantial population demands it.
	Language of Judiciary & Text of Law
	Article 348: English remains the official language in Supreme Court and High Court Unless otherwise provided. 
State Laws & Acts: Can be in state languages but must have an English translation. 
Governors (with Presidential consent) can allow state languages in High Court proceedings, but judgments must be in English unless Parliament decides otherwise. 
Official Language Act, 1963: Hindi translations of laws are authoritative but English remains primary. 
Supreme Court Proceedings: Only English is allowed for petitions.
	Special Directives
	Article 350: Right to submit grievances in any language used in the Union or State. 
Article 350A: States must ensure primary education in the mother tongue for linguistic minorities (President can issue directives). 
Article 350B: Special Officer for Linguistic Minorities appointed by the President to investigate and report to Parliament.
	Development of Hindi Language
	Article 351: The Union must promote Hindi as a lingua franca, assimilating forms, style, and expressions from other languages (especially Sanskrit and Hindustani). 
8th Schedule (22 languages): Originally 14, later additions include Sindhi, Konkani, Manipuri, Bodo, Santhali, Dogri, Maithili.
	Parliamentary Committee on Official Language
	Provision under Official Language Act: Consti every 10 years to review Hindi usage. 
Composition: 30 members (20 Lok Sabha, 10 Rajya Sabha). 
Duties: Submit reports to the President, who forwards them to State Governors. 
Chairman: Conventionally the Union Home Minister.
Inspect Central Government offices to promote Hindi adoption.
	8th Schedule (Language Additions)
	Order of additions: SKMN (Sindhi, Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali), BDMS (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali). 
Recent demands: Inclusion of Kumaoni, Garo, Khasi.
	Classical Language Status
	6 Classical Languages: Tamil, Sanskrit, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam, Odia (TeST OK M). 
Benefits: Financial aid for research, two major awards, UGC chairs for Classical Languages. 
Criteria: 1500+ years of recorded history, rich literary tradition, and distinct linguistic heritage.
	Public Services
	Three Categories: All India Services, Central Services, State Services.
	All India Services (AIS)
	IAS, IPS, IFS (Under All India Services Act, 1951). 
Controlled by Centre & State, but ultimate authority with the Central Government. 
Officers assigned to state cadres; can serve at the Centre on deputation.
	Central Services
	Direct control of the Central Government. Includes specialist, functional & technical roles. 
60 Group A Services (e.g., IFS, IRS). 
Highest Central Service = IFS, ranks next to IAS.
	State Services
	Controlled by State Governments. 
Gazetted (Group A, B) vs. Non-Gazetted (Group C, D). 
All India Services Act, 1951: Up to 33% posts filled by promotion from State Services (UPSC recommendation required).
	Constitutional Provisions for Civil Services
	Article 309: Parliament & State Legis regulate recruitment & service conditions. 
Includes: Pay, allowances, leave, promotions, tenure, termination, rights, and benefits.
	Office Tenure & Safeguards
	Article 310: Civil servants hold office at the pleasure of the President/Governor. 
Compensation for loss of office if: (1) Post abolished, or (2) Removed without misconduct. 
Article 311: Protection against arbitrary dismissal.
	Disciplinary Protections
	No dismissal/removal by subordinate authority. 
Exceptions: Criminal conviction, national security concerns, impracticality of inquiry. 
42nd Amendment: Removed the right of representation after punishment.
	All India Judicial Services
	Article 312: Parliament can create new services if Rajya Sabha passes a resolution by a two-thirds majority. 
Judicial Services should not include posts below District Judge. 
42nd Amendment introduced the provision, but no law has been enacted yet.
	Other Provisions
	Article 312A: Parliament can modify service conditions for pre-1950 civil servants. 
Article 313: Pre-1950 service laws continue until replaced. 
Article 314: Repealed protection for certain pre-1950 officers (28th Amendment Act).
	

Category
	Anti Defection
	Introduction
	52nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1985 added Tenth Schedule to curb defections and maintain political stability.
	Grounds for Disqualification
	MPs/MLAs can be disqualified if:
1. Voluntarily giving up party membership.
2. Voting/abstaining against party whip without prior approval. (Note- voting against party in presidential or Rajya sabha elections don’t count as defection)
3. Independent members joining a party after the election.
4. Nominated members joining a party after six months.
(No other condition)
	Exceptions (No Disqualification)
	Merger Clause: If two-thirds of a party’s legislators merge with another party, defection is not counted. (91st CAA)
Presiding Officers resigning from their party to maintain neutrality are not disqualified.
RS elections. Voting against a party is not defection. 
Presidential elections- conducted via secret ballot, so no party whip issued.
	Decision-making Authority
	Speaker/Chairman decides disqualification, subject to judicial review (SC ruling in Kihoto Hollohan Case, 1992). 
91st Amendment (2003): Disqualified members cannot become ministers until re-elected.
	Issues & Criticism (with Examples)
	Speaker’s Bias & Delays: Decision-making often favors the ruling party. 
Example: Manipur (2017-2020) – SC had to intervene after a long delay. 
Encourages Horse-Trading: MLAs resign, recontest, and become ministers, bypassing disqualification. 
Example: Karnataka (2019) – 17 MLAs resigned, toppling Congress-JD(S) govt. 
Suppresses Dissent: MPs/MLAs must follow party whip, limiting independent voting. 
Example: Julio Ribeiro Case (1989) – MP expelled for voting against party bill. 
Mass Defections Continue: Large-scale defections still destabilize governments. 
Example: Madhya Pradesh (2020) – Jyotiraditya Scindia & 22 MLAs shifted to BJP.
	Recent Developments (with Examples)
	Judicial Oversight: SC ruled Speaker’s decisions can be reviewed. 
Example: Manipur (2020) – SC ordered Speaker to act within 4 weeks. 
Resignation Tactic: Defectors resign instead of switching parties to avoid disqualification. 
Example: Maharashtra (2022) – Eknath Shinde’s revolt toppled Uddhav govt. 
Call for Independent Tribunal: SC & Law Commission recommend EC or Judiciary to decide defections. 
91st Amendment (2003): Removed bulk defections clause and barred defectors from ministerial posts.

	Recognition Criteria
	1. 6% valid votes in any 4 or more states in LS or SLA, + 4 LS seats
2. 2% of total LS seats (i.e., 11 seats) from at least 3 states — LS election
3. Recognised as a State Party in 4 or more states
	1. 6% valid votes in that SLA election + 2 seats in the Assembly
2. 6% valid votes in that State LS election + 1 Lok Sabha seat
3. 3% of total seats in SLA or minimum 3 seats, whichever is more 
4. 1 Lok Sabha seat per 25 Lok Sabha seats allotted to that state

Special Provisions for Certain Classes
	Rationale:
	                                                                  * Ensures equality & justice (Preamble)
                                                                  * Based on Article 330, 342 of the Constitution
	Categories
	                                                                  1. Permanent/Temporary – Some provisions are extended periodically.
                                                                  2. Protective/Developmental – Some prevent injustice, others promote socio-economic interests
	Specification of Classes:
	                                                                  * No caste/ tribe specified in the Constitution; President decides SC/ST status via notification.
                                                                  * Parliament alone can include/exclude caste/tribe, not the President.
                                                                  * Same rule for backward classes. (342 A).
                                                                  * Anglo- Indian definition: Male ancestor of European descent, domiciled in India
	Components of Special Provisions-
	                                                                  * SC/ ST reservations & special representation for Anglo-Indians. (based on population).
                                                                  * Initially for 10 years, extended repeatedly.
95th Amendment (2009) extended reservations till 2020.
	SC/ST in Services & Promotions:
	                                                                     * No compromise on administrative efficiency.
                                                                     * 82nd Amendment: Relaxation in exam marks & reservation in promotions.
                                                                     * Special grants & reservation for Anglo-Indians (ended in 1960).
The National Commission for SC/ST oversees welfare.
	Union Control Over SC/ST Welfare:
	                                                                        * Commissioner investigates backward classes' conditions & submits reports to Parliament.
                                                                        * Two commissions so far:
                                                                        * 1953 (Kaka Kalelkar): Rejected due to impractical recommendations.
                                                                        * 1970 (BP Mandal): Led to Mandal Commission Report (1980)
	

                Rights and Liabilities of Government (Articles 294-300, Part XII)
	Property of Union and State
	                                                                        * Succession: All property/assets of Dominion of India (before Constitution) now belong to Union/States.
                                                                        * Escheat & Bona Vacantia: Property without heir or owner vests with the state.
                                                                        * Sea Wealth: Land, minerals in territorial waters (12 NM), continental shelf, EEZ (200 NM) belong to the Union, not States.
                                                                        * Compulsory Acquisition:
                                                                        * Parliament/State legislatures can acquire private property.
                                                                        * 44th Amendment (1978): No compensation except for:
                                                                        1. Minority educational institutions
                                                                        2. Land under personal cultivation within ceiling limit
                                                                        * Executive Acquisition: Union can acquire, hold, and dispose of property under executive powers.
	Suits By/Against Government (Article 300)


	                                                                        * Torts (Wrongs by officials): Under Article 300, the government can be sued for torts in the name of the “Union of India” or the concerned “State”, just like the East India Company before independence.
                                                                        * The State is liable for non-sovereign (commercial/trading) acts, but not for sovereign functions—though this distinction has weakened over time through key court rulings.
	Liability for Contracts
	                                                                        * Contracts must be:
                                                                        1. Made by President/Governor
                                                                        2. Executed on their behalf
                                                                        3. Signed by an authorized officer
                                                                        * Officers not personally liable for government contracts.
	Suits Against Public Officials
	                                                                        * Official Acts:
                                                                        * President & Governor: Immunity for acts done in office, but can be reviewed by courts/parliament.
                                                                        * Personal Acts:
                                                                        * No criminal proceedings against the President /Governor during term.
                                                                        * Ministers: No immunity for personal acts; not liable for President/Governor's official acts.
                                                                        * Judicial Officers: Full immunity in judicial functions.
                                                                        * Civil Servants:
                                                                        * lmmunity for official duties (Govt liable) but not for personal acts.
                                                                        * 2-month advance notice needed for civil cases against them in official capacity.
	7th Schedule of the Constitution
List
	Categories & Subjects
	Union List (98 subjects)
	Defence & Security: Army, Navy, Air Force, Atomic Energy, Railway Protection Force, CAG, National Security. 
Foreign Affairs: Treaties, UN, Foreign Trade, Passports, Citizenship. 
Finance & Economy: RBI, Foreign Loans, Income Tax, Corporation Tax, Customs Duties, Stock Exchanges, Banking. 
Communication & Transport: Railways, Airways, Highways, Posts & Telegraphs, Wireless, Internet. 
Judiciary & Laws: Supreme Court, Official Secrets. Preventive detention for reasons of defence- foreign affairs- se 
Science & Technology: Space Research, Census, National Planning, Nuclear Energy. 
Miscellaneous: Inter-State Migration, Major Ports, National Highways, Institutions of National Importance.
	State List (59 subjects)
	Public Order & Police: Police, Public Order, Prisons, Local Courts, Officers of Village Administration. 
Health & Sanitation: Hospitals, Public Health, Water Supply, Drainage, Sanitation. 
Agriculture & Land: Land, Agriculture, Fisheries, Irrigation, Cooperative Societies, Agricultural Education. 
Finance & Trade: Land Revenue, Excise Duty on Alcohol, Taxes on Electricity, State Lotteries. 
Transport & Infrastructure: Roads, Inland Water Transport, Tolls, Ferries. 
Miscellaneous: Burials & Cremations, Pilgrimage (except foreign ones), Libraries, Gambling, Markets & Fairs.
	Concurrent List (52 subjects)
	Criminal Laws (IPC, CrPC, Evidence Act), Preventive Detention for security of state and public order, Marriage, Divorce, Adultery, Torts. 
Trade Unions, Social Security, Workmen’s Compensation, Industrial Disputes. 
Education, Newspapers, Books, Legal & Medical Professions. 
Forests, Protection of Wild Animals & Birds. 
Bankruptcy & Insolvency, Trusts, Weights & Measures, Factories, Price Control. 
Miscellaneous: Electricity, Population Control, Family Planning, Drugs, Lunacy, Charities, Ancient & Historical Monuments.
	

Aspect
	Details
	Origin & Concept
	Scandinavian Institute of Ombudsman (Swedish: Ombud = Legislature-appointed officer).
	Functions
	Redresses complaints against administration & judiciary.
	Issues Addressed
	Abuse of discretion, Maladministration & inefficiency, Corruption, Nepotism, Discourtesy/misconduct.
	Features of Ombudsman
	- Constitutional authority ensuring legality & fairness. 
- No suo moto power (acts on complaints). 
- Independent with unrestricted file access. 
- Reports to Legislature. 
- Transparent (media & public reporting). 
- Simple, informal, fast & cheap grievance handling.
	Global Models
	- UK: Parliamentary Commissioner for Administration. 
- France: Administrative Court System. 
- Socialist Nations: Procurator System.
	India: Lokpal & Lokayukta
	Administrative Reforms Commission (ARC) (1966-70) Recommendations:
- Lokpal (Centre) → Covers Ministers, Secretaries (excludes Judiciary). 
- Lokayukta (State level).
	Appointment & Qualifications
	Chairperson: CJI, SC Judge, or eminent public figure. 
Members (Max 8): 50% judicial, 50% SC/ST/OBC/Minority/Women. 
Non-judicial members: 25 years in vigilance, finance, anti-corruption, or public admin. 
Tenure: 5 years.
	Powers & Jurisdiction
	- Covers all public servants, NGOs receiving ₹10 lakh + donations. 
- States must set up Lokayukta within 1 year. 
- Supervises Central Investigative Agencies. 
- PM under Lokpal (along with Ministers & senior officials). 
- Recommends CBI Director selection (chaired by PM). 
- Prosecution Directorate under CBI (not Law Ministry). 
- CBI officers' transfer requires Lokpal’s approval. 
- Time-bound process: Inquiry (3 months + 3-month extension), Investigation (6 months + 6-month extension). 
- Power to sanction prosecution & confiscate assets of corrupt officials.
	Lokpal & Lokayukta Act, 2013
	- Drafted by Joint Committee (5 Ministers + 5 Team Anna nominees). 
- Enacted in 2014.
	Key Features
	- Uniform anti-corruption framework (Lokpal @ Centre, Lokayukta @ State). 
- Covers PM, MPs, Govt Officials (A, B, C, D categories). 
- 8-member Lokpal (50% judicial, 50% SC/ST/OBC/Minority/Women). 
- Selection by PM, Speaker, Opposition Leader, CJI nominee, eminent jurist. 
- CVC handles Grp A, B cases; Lokpal supervises CBI. 
- High-powered committee (PM led) selects CBI Director. 
- Asset attachment/ confiscation for corrupt officials. 
- CBI’s prosecution & legal autonomy: Independent Directorate of Prosecution, Private legal panels (not govt lawyers), Transfers only with Lokpal approval. 
- Foreign-funded entities (₹10L+ per year) under Lokpal. 
- Lokayukta appointed by the Governor (consulting HC CJ & Opposition Leader).
	Drawbacks
	- No suo moto powers (must act on complaints). 
- Focuses more on complaint format than substance. 
- Strict punishment for false complaints discourages reporting. 
- No anonymous complaints allowed. 
- Legal aid for accused officials (reduces Lokpal’s effectiveness). 
- 7-year limitation to file complaints. 
- Opaque process for PM-related complaints.

As per Article 124(7), a retired SC judge cannot appear before any court or authority in India. However, as per Article 220, a retired HC judge cannot practice in the same High Court where they served. However, they can practice in the Supreme Court or the other High Courts. Note- 1. Qualifications not mentioned for CEC, CAG, UPSC, national commission 2. No reappointment possible- CAG, CEC (within election com, silent for other jobs), UPSC  3.   govt offices are removed on same grounds and manner as SC are Judges of HC, CAG, CEC. 4. Parlia decides salaries and service conditions for CEC, CAG, FC.. rest prez
                                                                        1. Postal Ballots allowed in India- The Conduct of Election Rules, 1961, was amended in 2016 to allow only "service voters defined under RPA 1951" to use the ETPBS (electronically transmitted postal ballot system)
                                                                        1. Service Voters including wives (not husband)
                                                                        1. Armed Forces Personnel (Army, Navy, Air Force, etc.)
                                                                        2. Paramilitary Forces (CAPF like CRPF, BSF), BRO, Indian coast guard
                                                                        3. Police Personnel deployed on election duty in remote areas
                                                                        4. Only govt employees serving abroad (like embassy staff) qualify (not indian states)
                                                                        2. Voters Engaged in Essential Services- doctors, nurses, police
                                                                        3. Voters with Disabilities (40%)
                                                                        4. Senior Citizens (Above 80 years)
                                                                        5. preventive detention- allowed to vote on postal ballot 
                                                                        6. Not eligible
                                                                        1. Undertrials and Convicted persons are not allowed to vote in person or postal ballot.
                                                                        2. the convicted person on bail can vote in person but not postal ballot
                                                                        3. NRIs- They have to be physically present
                                                                        2. Electronic voting- 
                                                                        1. Service voters and their wives only (not husbands), other than those who opt for proxy voting
                                                                        2. 2 layer security- OTP + Pin
                                                                        3. Proxy Voting-  available only to service voters belonging to the Armed Forces or forces to which provisions of the Army Act, 1950, apply. (excluding state armed police forces)- Their wives are also not allowed.
